% Tolerate a partially-populated results directory: a missing table or figure
% becomes a visible placeholder rather than a failed build, so the manuscript
% can be proofread while the protocol is still running.
\newcommand{\inputtable}[1]{%
  \IfFileExists{../results/tables/#1.tex}{\input{../results/tables/#1}}%
  {\begin{table}[t]\centering\fbox{\texttt{\detokenize{#1}}: results pending}\end{table}}}
\newcommand{\resultfig}[2]{%
  \IfFileExists{../results/figures/#1.pdf}{\includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{#1.pdf}}%
  {\fbox{\parbox{0.9\linewidth}{\centering\vspace{2em}\texttt{\detokenize{#1}}: figure pending\vspace{2em}}}}}

\begin{center}
  \emph{Manuscript prepared for double-anonymous peer review. Author names,
  affiliations, and declarations appear on the separate title page.}
\end{center}
\vspace{0.5em}

\begin{abstract}
\noindent
Streaming visual-anomaly \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{systems --- illegal dumpingamong them --- are trained and evaluated as fixed-sample-size classifiers: given a short clip ,
emit one decision and report clip-level precision and recall. Deployment does
not look like that: a municipal camera runs for weeks, and what an operator
manages is the number of false alarms accumulated over the whole monitoring
period, because each one costs a dispatch. Alerting the first time a
per-frame threshold is crossed on a continuous stream }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{detectors, including those for illegal dumping, are evaluated per clip but deployed on continuous streams, where alerting at the first threshold crossing }\DIFaddend is optional stopping\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{,
and it inflates the true false-alarm rate by an amount clip-level evaluation
cannot show: }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. Fed valid 1\% p-values, }\DIFaddend a per-frame rule \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{fed p-values individually valid at the $1\%$
level raises at least one }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{raises a }\DIFaddend false alarm on \ValidityPfaPvaluePct\% of \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{$40$-minute }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{40-minute }\DIFaddend null streams. %DIF < 
\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Our contribution is an algorithm. Every anytime-valid sequential change
detector in use --- CUSUM, Shiryaev--Roberts, e-detectors}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{We introduce the episodic e-process}\DIFaddend , \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{non-partitioned
e-detectors --- models a switch that, once it happens, lasts forever. A
dumping event is an }\emph{\DIFdel{episode}}%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{: it begins, flickers under intermittent
occlusion, ends, and recurs weeks later at the same site. We replace the mixture over changepoint locations with }\DIFdelend a mixture over episodes \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, realised as
a two-state Markov chain whose forward recursion collapses a sum over $2^t$
trajectories into two numbers. The resulting }\emph{\DIFdel{episodic e-process}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{is an
exact }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{that start, flicker, end and recur. We prove that it is a }\DIFaddend non-negative supermartingale \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{when the p-values are conditionally super-uniform, }\DIFaddend so Ville's inequality gives time-uniform false-alarm control\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{with no new machinery --- costs $O(1)$ per
frame against $O(t)$ for a general mixture over active starts, and }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, and that it }\DIFaddend contains the changepoint mixture \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{exactly as the boundary case in which }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{as the case where }\DIFaddend episodes never end\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, a strict generalisation along an axis the anytime-valid e-process
family has left fixed at infinity. Around this core sits a per-camera
conformal layer calibrated conditionally on observable context and corrected
by exact Beta order-statistic }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. A two-state forward recursion evaluates it in constant time per frame on conformal p-values with exact calibration-conditional Beta }\DIFaddend levels, and \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{a fleet layer in which a graph
network steers each camera's episode hazard through a }\emph{\DIFdel{predictable}}
%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{modulation --- provably unable to damage the guarantee --- combined across
cameras by }\DIFdelend e-BH \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{under arbitrary dependence.
%DIF < 
Two evidence sources back these claims. Large-horizon simulated streams
($10^5$--$10^6$ frames) show every classical and parametric baseline we test
--- fixed threshold, }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{combines cameras. In simulation it raised no false alarm on 200 week-long streams, on which }\DIFaddend per-frame \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{p-value threshold, CUSUM, Shiryaev--Roberts, a
parametric e-detector, E-SHIFT --- failing to hold its nominal false-alarm
level, while SENTINEL-E and the changepoint mixture it strictly contains both
hold it ; a paired comparison on a shared betting grid resolves a real
advantage for the episodic mixture at two and four recurring episodes and a
real, smaller cost at one. We also report where the guarantee stops holding:
an unmodelled covariate shift beyond $\times\ShiftResidualBreaks$ inflates the
rate unless a shift-weighting safeguard is engaged, at a steep cost in power. %DIF < 
New in this revision: the pipeline is also run, unmodified, on real surveillance video (CUHK Avenue and }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{thresholds fail within an hour. Paired comparisons show episodes shorten detection delay for long, intermittent, recurring events but not for single or permanent changes. On real video (}\DIFaddend UCSD Ped2\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{) with a real }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, CUHK Avenue, Mivia-IWDD; }\DIFaddend optical-flow \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{backbone rather than a simulator. This surfaces a limitation the simulated
streams could not: real per-frame scores are autocorrelated across
hundreds of frames (\RealAvenueFullLag{} on Avenue, \RealPedTwoFullLag{} on
Ped2), close to or beyond a real episode's own duration, leaving only a
narrow lag window in which validity and power coexist at all. Inside that window the guarantee holds on both real
corpora --- realised false-alarm rate \RealPedTwoPfaHeadline{} on Ped2 and
\RealAvenuePfaHeadline{} on Avenue }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{and PatchCore backbones), operating points are chosen on training videos only and each test video is monitored alone. The thinned p-values remain serially dependent there, so validity is empirical rather than guaranteed. False-alarm proportions are consistent with the nominal level on the short single-camera corpora but exceed it in point estimate on Mivia-IWDD (9 of 50 normal videos }\DIFaddend at $\alpha=0.1$\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- while every classical
baseline tested fails to hold it on the same real streams; detection power is real but modest on Ped2 and weak on Avenue, whose real episodes are shorter.
This gap, not the simulated results, is the paper's central open problem. SENTINEL-E costs \CostSentinelUs\,}%DIFDELCMD < \textmu %%%
\DIFdel{s per frame, \CostSentinelPct\% of
the detector it wraps}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{). Detection power is limited and backbone-dependent, and classical baselines selected by the same rule perform comparably. The guarantee layer costs under two microseconds per frame}\DIFaddend .
\end{abstract}

\vspace{0.5em}
\noindent\textbf{Keywords:} video anomaly detection; illegal dumping\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{detection}\DIFdelend ; anytime-valid inference; e-values; conformal prediction; sequential change detection; \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{false discovery rate; video surveillance; edge deployment
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{video surveillance.
}\DIFaddend 

\section{Introduction}
\label{sec:intro}

Illegal dumping is a persistent municipal problem with an obvious
computer-vision framing\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, and the }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. The }\DIFaddend field has adopted that framing consistently: \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{collect clips, label
them }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{label
clips }\DIFaddend as containing a dumping event or not, train a \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{video
classifier , }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{classifier and }\DIFaddend report
clip-level accuracy. The 2026 \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{IWDD }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Illegal Waste Dumping Detection (IWDD) }\DIFaddend contest \citep{bouwmans2026overview} gave \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{the field }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{this
protocol }\DIFaddend a shared benchmark\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{on these terms:
ten }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. Ten }\DIFaddend teams trained on Mivia-IWDD (400 \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{surveillance videos, balanced
positive and
negative, }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{balanced
videos }\DIFaddend with onset timestamps) and were ranked on a private \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{$100$-video test set }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{test set of 100 videos}\DIFaddend .
Seven entries are published
\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{: X3D with temporal localisation
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{almorsy2026x3d}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
, cross-fold ensembles of Hiera and VideoMAE
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{kim2026kfold}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
, a three-headed classifier \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{scarrica2026garberus}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
, a
vision--language temporal analysis \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{kairanbay2026vlm}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
, a
temporal-spatial novelty detector \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{zbuce2026novelty}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
, onset-centric
boundary-sensitive estimation \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{bhat2026imslab}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
, and a lightweight
two-stage recurrent framework \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{putzu2026unicass}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
. Every one of them
reports per-video precision, recall and $F_1$ together with a notification-delay
measure, frame rate and peak memory, and the organisers' own overview
additionally analyses the field in ROC space with an explicit false-positive-rate
axis.
Where ``false alarms'' appear in an individual entry, they are counted
per clip--- }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{almorsy2026x3d,kim2026kfold,scarrica2026garberus,kairanbay2026vlm,zbuce2026novelty,bhat2026imslab,putzu2026unicass}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
.
Where they count false alarms, they count them per clip, as in }\DIFaddend ``six false alarms
on thirty negative videos'' \citep{almorsy2026x3d}\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- which }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. That }\DIFaddend is specificity, not a
rate over a monitoring period\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, and the same is
true of the organisers' ROC analysis: it is a per-clip quantity, not one over a
continuous horizon}\DIFdelend . The larger Mivia-IWDD-500 release
\citep{greco2026iwdd} \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{---
$250$ positive and $250$ negative surveillance videos with onset timestamps,
and the corpus this paper's protocol is built to run on --- }\DIFdelend postdates the contest\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{rather than having supplied it}\DIFdelend .

The framing does not match deployment. A camera \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{watching a lay-by does not receive a curated ten-second clip and }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{does not }\DIFaddend return a verdict \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{; it produces frames continuously }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{on a
curated clip. It produces frames }\DIFaddend for weeks, and the system alerts \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{the first time its
evidence }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{when its
evidence first }\DIFaddend crosses a threshold. \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{What the municipality manages is not per-clip precision but
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{The municipality manages }\DIFaddend the number of \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{enforcement dispatches wasted }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{wasted
enforcement dispatches }\DIFaddend over a season. \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Those two quantities
are separated by a step the clip-level protocol never exercises: }\emph{\DIFdel{repeatedly}}
%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{testing a stream }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Testing a stream repeatedly }\DIFaddend and stopping at
the first exceedance is optional stopping, and it invalidates the level at which
\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{any fixed }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{the }\DIFaddend threshold was calibrated.

The \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{magnitude of the gap is easy to state and, we find, easy to underestimate. A rule that raises a false alarm on each frame }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{gap is larger than it looks. A per-frame rule that false-alarms }\DIFaddend with
probability $q$ raises at least one \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{alarm }\DIFaddend over $T$ frames with probability
$1-(1-q)^{T}$, which tends to one for any $q>0$. \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Persistence filters and score smoothing change $q$; they do not change
the limit. Feeding a per-frame }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Feed that }\DIFaddend rule p-values that are
\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{individually and provably
}\DIFdelend valid at the $1\%$ level\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- so that nothing is wrong with the calibration ---
produces }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. The probability of }\DIFaddend at least one false alarm on \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{\ValidityPfaPvaluePct\% of }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{a }\DIFaddend $40$-minute null \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{streams }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{stream, the false-alarm probability (PFA), is then \ValidityPfaPvaluePct\% }\DIFaddend (\cref{fig:validity}\,(a))\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{; a raw score }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. A
raw }\DIFaddend threshold tuned to a $10^{-3}$ per-frame rate reaches \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{\HorizonFixedPfaShort{} within \HorizonMinHours\,hours and \HorizonFixedPfa{} by \HorizonMaxHours\,hours (\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{fig:validity}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
\,(b)}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{a false-alarm probability
of $0.980$ within one hour and $1.000$ from six hours (\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{tab:horizon_week}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
}\DIFaddend ). A
clip-level evaluation \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{cannot see this, because it }\DIFdelend never runs the detector for \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{two hours}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{hours, so it cannot see this}\DIFaddend .

Nothing \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{in the argument above }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{here }\DIFaddend is specific to dumping. \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Any scene-level
anomaly a per-frame backbone scores --- loitering, wrong-direction motion ,
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Loitering, wrong-way motion or }\DIFaddend an abandoned
object \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, a vehicle where pedestrians belong --- exposes }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{faces }\DIFaddend the same gap\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{between a per-clip evaluation and a continuously monitored stream, and
the method that follows is built and validated at that scope: an
anytime-valid sequential detector for }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. The method therefore targets }\DIFaddend streaming visual anomaly
surveillance, with \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{illegal }\DIFdelend dumping as the motivating case\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{throughout. We say this plainly
rather than let it surface only in the data section: \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:real-data}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
's
empirical validation runs on two classical video-anomaly-detection corpora, not dumping footage,
because no continuously-labelled real dumping stream of
the required length is publicly available, and that scope mismatch is a real limitation this paper discloses }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. Real-data validation covers UCSD
Ped2 and CUHK Avenue, which are pedestrian-walkway corpora, and Mivia-IWDD-500,
which contains illegal-dumping footage. Each corpus is run with an optical-flow and
a PatchCore backbone, and each test video is monitored alone. The Mivia labels give
onset times only, and the real streams are short. The long-horizon claims therefore
rest on simulation }\DIFaddend (\cref{sec:limitations})\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{rather than hides}\DIFdelend .

\subsection{Why the existing sequential machinery is the wrong shape}

The tools for optional stopping are mature. Ville's inequality \citep{ville1939}
bounds the probability that a non-negative supermartingale \DIFdelbegin \emph{\DIFdel{ever}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{ever }\DIFaddend exceeds a level\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, and the game-theoretic literature
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{shafer2019game,ramdas2023savi,grunwald2024evalues} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
turns that into a
construction: bet }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{.
Betting }\DIFaddend against the null \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, and }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{turns }\DIFaddend the accumulated wealth \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{is }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{into }\DIFaddend a time-uniform
certificate \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{. Conformal prediction \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{vovk2005alrw} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
supplies distribution-free
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{shafer2019game,ramdas2023savi}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
. Conformal }\DIFaddend p-values
\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{to bet on, e-detectors }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{vovk2005alrw} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
supply the bets. E-detectors }\DIFaddend \citep{shin2024edetectors} adapt
the machinery to change detection, \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{non-partitioned e-detectors \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{saha2026nonpartitioned}
}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
sharpen it to composite pre-change classes, }\DIFdelend and e-BH \citep{wang2022ebh} combines evidence
across streams\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{under arbitrary dependence. To our knowledge none of it
has been applied }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. We know of no application of this machinery }\DIFaddend to waste, dumping or
pollution surveillance.

Importing it unchanged would not be enough\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, and the reason is structural rather
than a matter of tuning. }\emph{\DIFdel{Every one of these procedures models a persistent
change.}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. Every one of these procedures models a
persistent change. }\DIFaddend At an unknown frame $\nu$ the law switches from $P_0$ to $P_1$
and stays there\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{for the rest of time; the mixtures are }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, and the mixtures run }\DIFaddend over $\nu$ alone\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, and the optimality
theory, thresholds and delay bounds all presume it}\DIFdelend .

An illegal dumping event is \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{not a persistent change. It is }\DIFdelend an \emph{episode}. A van pulls up, the offender unloads
for forty seconds and drives away. \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Inside }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Within }\DIFaddend the episode the signal is \DIFdelbegin \emph{\DIFdel{intermittent}}%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{intermittent}\DIFaddend :
the offender is occluded\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{by the
vehicle, steps out of frame }\DIFdelend , \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{leaves the frame and }\DIFaddend returns. Then the \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{episode ends, the }\DIFdelend stream reverts to
\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{its nullbehaviour, and --- because a site that has been }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{null. A site }\DIFaddend used once is \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{used
again --- another episode follows }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{often used again }\DIFaddend weeks later.

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Modelling that as a persistent change is not a small }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{A persistent-change model pays for this }\DIFaddend mismatch. A changepoint-mixture wealth
process keeps betting at full aggression after the episode \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{is over, handing back what the episode earned; and because it treats
every frame after $\nu$ as drawn from the post-change law, }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{ends. It also counts }\DIFaddend the
quiet frames \DIFdelbegin \emph{\DIFdel{inside}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{the episode count against it rather than being expected}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{inside the episode against the alternative}\DIFaddend . Both effects lengthen
delay, and the second \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{worsens as intermittencyincreases ---
which is the regime this problem lives in. Empirically, over \EpiMaxCount{}
recurring episodes per camera the changepoint mixture misses \EpiManyCpMiss{}
of events where the episodic construction misses \EpiManyEpiMiss{}; at
\EpiMidCount{} episodes , a smaller but still substantial gap , it is
\EpiMidCpMiss{} against \EpiMidEpiMiss{} (\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:episodes}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
). }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{grows with intermittency. In paired simulations on grids of equal
size, the episodic mixture reduces the mean censored delay by 141.1\,s (95\% CI +79.6
to +210.2\,s) for four recurring 1600-frame episodes with $\pi=0.2$ and a mean gap of
12}{\DIFadd{,}}\DIFadd{000 frames (\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{tab:corner_sweep}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
). A single episode reverses the sign, by 55.7\,s (95\% CI 14.0 to
111.2\,s) in favour of the changepoint mixture (\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{tab:corner_sweep}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
). \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:episodes} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
maps the regimes.
}\DIFaddend 

\subsection{Contributions}

\DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{The paper makes three kinds of contribution: theory, an algorithm and an
evaluation method.
}

\DIFaddend \begin{itemize}
\item \DIFdelbegin \textbf{\DIFdel{The episodic e-process}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{(\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:episodic}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
), our main
      contribution. }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \textbf{\DIFadd{Theory: an episodic e-process.}} \DIFaddend Mixing over episodes\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{rather than changepoints, via a
      two-state Markov chain, gives a wealth process that is (i) }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, not
      changepoints, gives }\DIFaddend an exact non-negative supermartingale, so Ville's
      inequality applies unchanged \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{;
      (ii) computable in $O(1)$ time and memory per frame by an HMM forward
      recursion, where a general mixture over active starts costs $O(t)$ per
      observation \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{saha2026nonpartitioned}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
; and (iii)a strict
      generalisation, equal
      to }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{(\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{thm:episodic}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
). It equals }\DIFaddend the changepoint
      mixture exactly when the episode-end rate is zero (\cref{prop:generalisation}).

\item \textbf{\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{A quantity worth passing between cameras, whose one tested use
      did not pay off}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Theory: exact conformal levels}\DIFaddend .} \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{The same recursion returns the posterior probability
      that a camera is currently inside an episode. Unlike accumulated wealth
      it is bounded in $[0,1]$ and comparable across cameras with different
      histories, which is the property a message-passing layer needs to
      aggregate it without one long-running camera swamping its neighbours ---
      and \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:network} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
reports, as a negative result, that the one
      spatial-prior architecture we built on top of it does not shorten
      detection delay, so on the fleets tested here the graph layer's measured
      value comes entirely from e-BH at the combination step, not from this
      quantity. The property the posterior offers a future architecture is
      real; the specific use we made of it is not one of the paper's positive
      results.
}%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < \item %%%
\item%DIFAUXCMD
\textbf{\DIFdel{Exact calibration-conditional conformal levels}}
      %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{(\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{thm:beta}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
). }\DIFdelend Each attainable p-value level is
      replaced by its exact Beta order-statistic \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{upper confidence bound rather than inflated
      additively. At
      $n=\CalN{}$, $\delta=\CalDelta{}$ }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{bound (\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{thm:beta}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
). At
      $n=\CalN$ and $\delta=\CalDelta$, }\DIFaddend the smallest usable p-value \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{becomes $\BetaPmin{}$ instead of the $\DkwPmin{}$ that
      Dvoretzky--Kiefer--Wolfowitz permits --- a }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{falls from
      $\DkwPmin$ to $\BetaPmin$, an improvement of }\DIFaddend $\BetaOverDkwSpeedup\times$ \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{improvement precisely where a betting detector earns its wealth, worth a
      factor of \AblDkwSpeedup{} in detection delay}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{over
      the Dvoretzky--Kiefer--Wolfowitz bound}\DIFaddend .

\item \textbf{\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{A learned spatial prior that provably cannot break the
      guarantee}%DIFDELCMD < \MBLOCKRIGHTBRACE %%%
\DIFdel{(\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{thm:predictable}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
)--- and the measurement showing it does
      not help either.
      Because the network's output }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Algorithm: a constant-time recursion.}} \DIFadd{A forward recursion runs the
      mixture in $O(1)$ time and memory per frame (\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{eq:forward,alg:episodic}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
).
      Direct evaluation over active starts costs $O(t)$. The guarantee layer adds
      under two microseconds per frame (\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:compute}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
).
}

\item \textbf{\DIFadd{A learned prior and a posterior, with a negative result.}} \DIFadd{A learned
      spatial prior }\DIFaddend enters only as a predictable modulation\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{of the episode hazard and the betting stake, the wealth remains
      a supermartingale }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, so the guarantee holds
      }\DIFaddend whatever it outputs \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{. Our experiments confirm the theorem
      and refute the motivation: the learned controller leaves the false-alarm
      guarantee exactly intact while }\emph{\DIFdel{reducing}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{power
      (\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:network}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
). We report the construction because the invariance is
      worth having --- it is what lets a learned component sit on top of a formal
      guarantee at all --- and the result because it is what we found}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{(\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{thm:predictable}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
). The posterior probability of
      being inside an episode is bounded in $[0,1]$. The spatial-prior architecture
      built on it did not shorten delay (\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:network}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
)}\DIFaddend .

\item \textbf{An evaluation \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{that measures }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{of }\DIFaddend guarantees\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{rather than asserting
      them}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{.}\DIFaddend } \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{We report realised false-alarm rates
      }\DIFaddend over up to \ExpOneReps{} \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Monte-Carlo }\DIFdelend streams per operating point, against six baselines\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{including a reimplementation of the closest generic
      prior art \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{khan2026eshift}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
; a component ablation in which
      \AblNumInvalid{} of \AblNumVariants{} variants fail to deliver the level
      they claim; and a cost measurement putting the guarantee layer at
      \CostSentinelPct\% of its backbone. We also correct a measurement pitfall
      that affects such comparisons generally: detection delay conditioned on
      detection is averaged over a different subset of runs for each method and
      systematically flatters the ones that miss the hard events, so we report a
      censored delay alongside it }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{,
      including E-SHIFT \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{khan2026eshift}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
. Delay is reported censored as well as
      conditionally }\DIFaddend (\cref{sec:problem}). \DIFdelbegin %DIFDELCMD < \item %%%
\item%DIFAUXCMD
\textbf{\DIFdel{A negative result we would have preferred not to have.}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{We
      introduced a dilated betting function to model within-episode
      intermittency, and the ablation shows the $\kappa$ mixture already
      handles it: dilation costs delay and never repays it
      (\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:dilation}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
).
      It is off by default and reported rather than
      buried}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Real video is monitored per video, with
      operating points chosen on training videos only (\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:real-data}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
).
      Dilated betting did not help (\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:dilation}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
)}\DIFaddend .
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{What we do not claim.}
The changepoint mixture with retained tail mass \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{that our construction reduces to
}\DIFdelend is not new\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{: it }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. It }\DIFaddend is the
PFA-controlled e-detector of \citet{saha2026nonpartitioned}, which \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{already delivers
$\mathbb{P}(\tau < \infty) \le \alpha$ for a mixture over start times with
weights summing to one}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{gives
$\mathbb{P}(\tau<\infty)\le\alpha$}\DIFaddend . Our contribution is the episode axis on top of
it, and the $O(1)$ recursion that \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{axis happens to make available. The conformal }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{makes that axis cheap. Conformal prediction }\DIFaddend and
e-BH \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{layers }\DIFdelend are existing machinery\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, used as intended and credited as such;
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:limitations} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
states plainly }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:limitations} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
sets out }\DIFaddend what the evaluation
does and does not establish.

\section{Related work}
\label{sec:related}

\paragraph{Waste and dumping detection.}
\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Image-level litter detection is well served by TACO \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{proenca2020taco}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
;
industrial waste-sorting imagery, a related but distinct acquisition regime of
cluttered conveyor-belt scenes rather than in-the-wild litter, by ZeroWaste \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{bashkirova2022zerowaste}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
. UAV and wide-area monitoring is served by
UAVVaste \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{kraft2021uavvaste}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
and AerialWaste \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{torres2023aerialwaste}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
; marine pollution by }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{TACO \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{proenca2020taco}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
, ZeroWaste \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{bashkirova2022zerowaste}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
,
UAVVaste \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{kraft2021uavvaste}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
, AerialWaste \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{torres2023aerialwaste}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
,
}\DIFaddend MARIDA \citep{kikaki2022marida} and MADOS \citep{kikaki2024mados} \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{.
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{are image,
aerial, satellite or industrial datasets. None is a continuous video stream.
}\DIFaddend 

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Video-based dumping detection now has a public benchmark and an active contest
around it.
}\DIFdelend The 2026 IWDD contest \citep{bouwmans2026overview} \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{drew ten teams ,
trained on the released }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{ranked ten teams on }\DIFaddend Mivia-IWDD \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{set (400 videos, 200 positive/200 negative)
and ranked on a private 100-video test set }\DIFdelend by
precision, recall\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{and F1 plus
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, $F_1$, }\DIFaddend normalised notification delay, \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{processing }\DIFdelend frame rate and peak memory\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{; the
larger Mivia-IWDD-500 release we build our protocol on \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{greco2026iwdd}
}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
postdates the contest rather than having supplied it.
Seven of the ten }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{.
Seven }\DIFaddend entries are published: 3D CNNs with temporal localisation
\citep{almorsy2026x3d}, \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{cross-fold }\DIFdelend transformer ensembles \citep{kim2026kfold}, multi-head
classifiers \citep{scarrica2026garberus}, vision--language \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{temporal }\DIFdelend reasoning
\citep{kairanbay2026vlm}, novelty detection \citep{zbuce2026novelty},
onset-centric \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{boundary-sensitive estimation \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{bhat2026imslab} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
, and a lightweight }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{estimation \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{bhat2026imslab} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
and a }\DIFaddend two-stage recurrent framework
\citep{putzu2026unicass}. \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{These are
strong backbones and our work does not compete with them: }\DIFdelend SENTINEL-E consumes a frozen backbone's scores, so \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{a better backbone and this work compound. What
none }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{it
complements these entries. None }\DIFaddend of them reports \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- and what the clip-level protocol they share cannot
express --- is }\DIFdelend a false-alarm rate over a
continuous \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{monitoring }\DIFdelend horizon, an average run length \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, or a detection }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{or a }\DIFaddend delay under continuous monitoring\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{; the
contest overview does analyse the field in ROC space with an explicit
false-positive-rate axis, which is still a per-clip quantity rather than a
rate over a horizon, so the distinction we draw survives with that
qualification attached.
}%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\paragraph{\DIFdel{Video anomaly detection benchmarks (2025--2026).}}
%DIFAUXCMD
\addtocounter{paragraph}{-1}%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{The classical video anomaly detection (VAD) literature this paper's }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{.
The contest's ROC analysis is also per clip. Our }\DIFaddend real-data protocol \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{draws its streams from --- }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{uses the larger
Mivia-IWDD-500 release \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{greco2026iwdd}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
.
}

\paragraph{\DIFadd{Video anomaly detection benchmarks.}}
\DIFaddend CUHK Avenue \citep{lu2013avenue} and UCSD Ped2 \citep{mahadevan2010ucsd} \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- continues to report clip- or }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{are
classical benchmarks, and recent work reports }\DIFaddend frame-level \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{ROC-AUC as its primary metric: a }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{area under the ROC curve (AUC) on them. One
}\DIFaddend reconstruction-based \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{idempotent generative
network reaches }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{method reports }\DIFaddend 99.03\% AUC on Ped2 and 92.40\% on Avenue
\citep{dong2025rvadign}. \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{That method post-dates this paper's original
submission and outperforms, on frame-level AUC alone, the Farneback
optical-flow backbone \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:real-data} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
uses
(\RealPedTwoAuc{}/\RealAvenueAuc{}). We do not run it head to head against
SENTINEL-E, and the reason is the point this paper makes rather than an
omission: }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{For a survey, see \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citet{liu2024generalized}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
. }\DIFaddend AUC is
computed after the fact \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{over a fixed, already-labelled test setand says nothing about the rate at which a
rule raises an alarm on a
continuously monitored, }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{on a labelled test set. It does not measure how often a
rule alarms on an }\DIFaddend unlabelled stream, which is \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{the quantity
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:real-data} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
actually }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{what our protocol }\DIFaddend measures.
\citet{rashidi2026benchmarkauc} \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{audits
this gap directly: eleven off-the-shelf VAD feature sets that }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{show the consequence. Off-the-shelf feature sets from
four backbones, evaluated on four datasets, }\DIFaddend average 70.4\% AUC on their \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{own training
distribution collapse }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{training
distribution and fall }\DIFaddend to 49.9\%\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- chance--- under a }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, which is chance, under }\DIFaddend cross-dataset shift\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, with }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. At an
operating point that reaches 0.9 recall on the source dataset, the }\DIFaddend false-alarm
\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{rates around $3.2\times 10^4$ per hourat thresholds a same-dataset evaluation would call operational.
That finding
and \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:real-data}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
's own (the narrow, dataset-specific lag window a }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{rate is around $3.2\times 10^{4}$ per hour.
}

\DIFadd{Our real-data backbones are optical flow, estimated by Farneb\"{a}ck's method
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{farneback2003}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
, and PatchCore. PatchCore scores each patch by its
nearest-neighbour distance to a memory bank of frozen ImageNet features from a
residual network \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{roth2022total,he2016deep}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
.
}

\paragraph{\DIFadd{Online detection with a false-alarm bound.}}
\DIFadd{The closest prior work is \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citet{doshi2021online}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
. It gives an online detector for
surveillance video with an asymptotic bound on the false-alarm rate. Our guarantee
is non-asymptotic and }\DIFaddend time-uniform\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{guarantee actually needs on real footage) are independent
audits of the same failure mode from opposite ends: a
benchmark-AUC number
does not certify what happens when the clock keeps running}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, so it holds at all monitoring times at once.
Doshi and Yilmaz also treat video anomaly detection as continual learning
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{doshi2022rethinking}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
}\DIFaddend .

\paragraph{Sequential detection of changes that end.}
\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Classical quickest-detection theory treats a permanent shift}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Quickest-detection theory mostly treats permanent shifts}\DIFaddend , but a sub-literature
treats \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{exactly the object this paper does: a change of finite ,
possibly unknown }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{changes of finite }\DIFaddend duration. Transient change detection
\citep{guepie2012transient,guepie2017transient} minimises worst-case missed
detection under a false-alarm constraint\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{for a change that must be caught
before it disappears, using a window-limited CUSUM; the intermittent-change
line \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{sokolov2023intermittent} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
treats a change of }\emph{\DIFdel{unknown}}
%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{duration
directly, and }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. Intermittent changes of unknown duration
are treated in \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citet{sokolov2023intermittent}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
. \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citet{fuh2015hmm} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
use }\DIFaddend a two-state
hidden Markov model\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{is used for quickest
detection in \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citet{fuh2015hmm} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
--- structurally }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, }\DIFaddend the closest classical relative of the \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{two-state }\DIFdelend chain in
\cref{eq:episodic}. Epidemic changepoint \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{detection
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{fisch2022capa,juodakis2020epidemic}
}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
treats a related but distinct
problem, }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{methods \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{fisch2022capa,juodakis2020epidemic}
}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
handle }\DIFaddend a segment that departs from and \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{then }\DIFdelend returns to a background level\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{estimated from the same series, going back to the original two-changepoint
epidemic-alternative formulation. None of this literature is ARL- or
Ville-type time-uniform: it is CUSUM-, SPRT- or window-shaped }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. These methods
use CUSUM, SPRT or window statistics }\DIFaddend with asymptotic or \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{fixed-sample-size guarantees, not an e-process.
What we add is not the
finite-duration change itself but }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{fixed-sample guarantees.
None is time-uniform. We add }\DIFaddend an anytime-valid, conformal-fed construction of \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{it; \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:episodic} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
states that scope explicitly rather than claiming
the
axis itself is new}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{the
finite-duration change. The finite-duration change itself is not new, and
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:episodic} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
states that scope}\DIFaddend .

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{On the construction: mixing a bounded-wealth process }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Mixing }\DIFaddend over the trajectories of a two-state chain \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{and collapsing them by a forward pass is an instance of }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{belongs to }\DIFaddend the fixed-share, or
switching-expert, family \citep{herbster1998fixedshare,vanerven2012switch}\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, of which
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{eq:episodic} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
is a two-expert case with one expert held at the neutral
bet. What that }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. That
}\DIFaddend literature does not \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{supply is }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{give }\DIFaddend the martingale property under optional stopping\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{that \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{thm:episodic} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
establishes; conversely, what we do
not claim is that mixing over a switching structure is itself new. }%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, which
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{thm:episodic} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
establishes. }\DIFaddend Closer to our architecture \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{than either of these }\DIFdelend are the conformal test
martingales of \citet{vovk2003exchangeability}\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{and their successors, which
combine }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. They pair }\DIFaddend a conformal front end with
a Ville-thresholded martingale for \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{online
}\DIFdelend change and exchangeability testing\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- the same two-layer shape as
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:layer1,sec:episodic}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
. We found no instance of that combination, nor of any other }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. We know of no
}\DIFaddend e-process or anytime-valid conformal \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{machinery, }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{method }\DIFaddend applied to waste, dumping \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, }\DIFdelend or pollution
surveillance\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{specifically, though classical sequential
change detection has reached adjacent environmental problems, including
contamination detection in water-distribution networks
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{guepie2012watercontamination}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
}\DIFdelend .

\paragraph{Sequential change detection.}
CUSUM \citep{page1954}, Shiryaev's \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Bayesian }\DIFdelend statistic \citep{shiryaev1963} and
Shiryaev--Roberts \citep{roberts1966} are the classical procedures, with optimality
\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{theory }\DIFdelend due to \citet{lorden1971} and \citet{moustakides1986}. \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{A full treatment is in
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citet{tartakovsky2014sequential}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
. }\DIFaddend Their guarantees are asymptotic
\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{average-run-length statements presuming }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{statements about the average run length (ARL) under }\DIFaddend a correctly specified pre-change law\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{; detector scores from real footage }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. Footage
scores }\DIFaddend are autocorrelated, heavy-tailed and diurnally non-stationary, \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{so the presumption fails and both
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{and both
procedures }\DIFaddend lose control in our experiments. \citet{shin2024edetectors} give a
nonparametric e-detector framework with non-asymptotic ARL bounds\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, and
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. }\DIFaddend \citet{saha2026nonpartitioned}
extend it to composite pre-change classes\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{,
including a }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. Their }\DIFaddend PFA-controlled variant \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{that }\DIFdelend mixes over
start times with weights summing to one\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{and thereby achieves $\mathbb{P}(\tau<\infty) \le \alpha$.
That
variant is exactly what our }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, which gives $\mathbb{P}(\tau<\infty)\le\alpha$.
}\DIFaddend \cref{prop:generalisation} reduces to \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, and we claim
no credit for it}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{that variant at zero episode-end rate}\DIFaddend .

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{The difference is the change model. }\DIFdelend All of these mix over the changepoint \DIFdelbegin \emph{\DIFdel{location}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{location }\DIFaddend of a persistent shift\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, and none of them is an
e-process: what
they lack is not the finite-duration axis itself, which classical
quickest-detection theory has treated since the transient- and
intermittent-change literature discussed above, but }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. None adds an
episode-end axis with }\DIFaddend a time-uniform, optional-stopping-safe \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{construction of it that stays $O(1)$ per frame and feeds
from a conformal front end rather than a parametric one. That is the axis
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:episodic} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
adds }\emph{\DIFdel{to this family}}%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{. A secondary but practical
difference is cost}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{guarantee. Cost also
separates them}\DIFaddend . Evaluated exactly \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{across }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{over }\DIFaddend all active starts, a non-partitioned
e-detector \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{is }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{costs }\DIFaddend $O(t)$ per observation \citep{saha2026nonpartitioned}\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{; under }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. Under }\DIFaddend a
geometric prior over start times\DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, }\DIFaddend the sum telescopes to \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{an }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{the }\DIFaddend $O(1)$ recursion \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, which is the special case }\DIFdelend of
\cref{eq:forward} at $\eta=0$. The \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{point is that the episodic process retains
that $O(1)$ cost }\emph{\DIFdel{while adding the episode-end axis to the e-process
family specifically}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{--- the extra generality is free arithmetically as well
as statistically, which matters when the horizon is a month of video and the
hardware is a camera}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{episodic process keeps that cost while adding the
episode-end axis}\DIFaddend .

\paragraph{Anytime-valid inference and e-values.}
Testing by betting \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{shafer2021testing,ramdas2023savi,grunwald2024evalues}
}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{shafer2021testing} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
}\DIFaddend turns Ville's inequality into a design
principle\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{; }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, and }\DIFaddend \citet{waudby2024betting} develop the betting strategies we adapt.
\citet{wang2022ebh} introduce e-BH, which controls \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{FDR }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{the false discovery rate (FDR) }\DIFaddend under
arbitrary dependence and underpins our fleet layer\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{;
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citet{vovk2021evalues} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
characterise e-value merging. Closest to this }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. The closest }\DIFaddend work in spirit is
E-SHIFT \citep{khan2026eshift}, \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{which detects }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{a sub-exponential e-process for }\DIFaddend distribution shift
in streaming \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{learning systemswith a sub-exponential e-process built on a
composite non-conformity score, and which already provides an $\alpha$-mixing
extension for temporally correlated streams}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{systems}\DIFaddend . We reimplement it as a baseline. It differs \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{from SENTINEL-E }\DIFdelend in three ways\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{that matter here: its
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{.
Its }\DIFaddend log-moment-generating-function bound is estimated, so by the authors' \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{own
}\DIFdelend account
validity is asymptotic in the calibration size\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{rather than
finite-sample; it }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. It }\DIFaddend assumes i.i.d.\ calibration and
deployment scores, which is stronger than the exchangeability our conformal layer
needs\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{and which }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, and }\DIFaddend a diurnal cycle breaks \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{regardless; and its wealth starts at frame one }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{it. Its wealth restarts }\DIFaddend after each alert, so
evidence arriving hours into a stream must first repay what was burned \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{before it, exactly as our own restart convention does between alarms }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{earlier. Our
restart convention between alarms has the same effect}\DIFaddend .

\paragraph{Conformal prediction.}
Split conformal prediction \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{vovk2005alrw} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{vovk2005alrw,angelopoulos2023gentle} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
}\DIFaddend supplies the
distribution-free p-values we bet on. \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Conformal p-values also test for outliers
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{bates2023testing}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
, and exchangeability can be relaxed for sequential data
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{barber2023beyond,xu2023sequential}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
. }\DIFaddend \citet{vovk2012conditional} studies
calibration-conditional validity, which \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{is the concern }\DIFdelend our Beta levels address\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, and
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citet{tibshirani2019covariate} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
handle covariate shift }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. Covariate shift is
handled }\DIFaddend by likelihood-ratio weighting \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{tibshirani2019covariate}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
}\DIFaddend , which we
implement and evaluate. \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Conformalising a normalised
regression residual is standard practice
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{lei2018distribution,romano2019cqr}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
, and localisation
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{guan2023localized} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
pursues }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Conformalised regression residuals \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{lei2018distribution}
}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
pursue }\DIFaddend conditional coverage by other means. \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Our }\DIFdelend Layer 1 \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{combines these with }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{adds }\DIFaddend the sequential requirement\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, which imposes a constraint the
prediction-interval literature does not face: the }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{.
The }\DIFaddend p-values must be super-uniform \DIFdelbegin \emph{\DIFdel{conditionally on the past}}%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{conditionally on the past}\DIFaddend , not merely marginally,
or the wealth process is not a supermartingale.

\DIFaddbegin \paragraph{\DIFadd{Control charts and ROC practice.}}
\DIFadd{Control charts are tuned to an in-control average run length, and their limits are
revisited when the process changes \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{montgomery2020introduction}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
. Our
time-uniform false-alarm probability is the horizon-free analogue: it bounds the
chance of any alarm at any time. ROC analysis fixes a per-decision operating point
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{fawcett2006introduction}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
. A budget of false alarms per monitoring period
depends on how many decisions are made, which a per-decision point does not show.
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:limitations} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
returns to alarm budgets.
}

\DIFaddend \section{Method}
\label{sec:method}

\subsection{Problem formulation}
\label{sec:problem}

A camera produces frames $x_1, x_2, \ldots$\DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, }\DIFaddend and a frozen detector $g$ produces scores $s_t = g(x_t) \in \mathbb{R}$, with higher meaning more dumping-like. \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Alongside each frame we observe }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Each frame also yields }\DIFaddend a low-dimensional context vector $c_t \in \mathbb{R}^d$\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{: }\DIFaddend time of day, a weather flag, ambient luminance \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{and }\DIFaddend a background-subtraction motion measure\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- available to any deployed camera }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. Any deployed camera can observe these }\DIFaddend without privileged knowledge. The operator supplies a per-camera calibration set $\cD = \{(s_i^{\mathrm{cal}}, c_i^{\mathrm{cal}})\}_{i=1}^{n}$ of confirmed no-dumping frames.

Write $\Pinf$ for the law of the stream under ``no dumping ever'' and $\mathbb{P}_{\nu}$ for the law when dumping begins at frame $\nu$. A detection rule is a stopping time $\tau$ with respect to the observation filtration $(\Fcal_t)$. We require:

\begin{definition}[Time-uniform false-alarm control]
\label{def:tuv}
A rule has time-uniform false-alarm level $\alpha$ if
$\Pinf(\tau < \infty) \le \alpha$.
\end{definition}

The quantifier is the whole point. A per-frame guarantee $\Pinf(\text{alarm at } t) \le \alpha$ for each fixed $t$ says nothing about $\Pinf(\exists t : \text{alarm at } t)$, and the gap \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{between them }\DIFdelend grows with the horizon. \Cref{def:tuv} is what a municipality \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{is implicitly buying when it is
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{buys when }\DIFaddend told a detector has a $1\%$ false-alarm rate.

Dumping is episodic, so we write $\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{E}}$ for the law when a set of episodes $\mathcal{E} = \{[\nu_1, \nu_1 + L_1], \ldots\}$ occurs, within each of which a fraction $\pi$ of frames are anomalous. The \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{single-persistent-change
model }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{single persistent change }\DIFaddend is the special case of one episode with $L = \infty$ and $\pi = 1$.

We report, over \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Monte-Carlo }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Monte Carlo }\DIFaddend replicates: $\PFA$, the \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{false-alarm probability: the }\DIFaddend probability that a null stream of a stated length raises at least one alarm; $\ARL$, the \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{average run length: the }\DIFaddend mean frames to a false alarm on a null stream, censored at the horizon; $\ADD$, the \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{average detection delay: the }\DIFaddend mean frames from the first onset $\nu_1$ to the first alarm at or after it, over runs that alarm; and the miss rate, the fraction of post-change segments ending unflagged.

\paragraph{A measurement pitfall, and the fix.}
Conditional $\ADD$ is averaged over a \DIFdelbegin \emph{\DIFdel{different subset of runs for each
method}}%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{: a }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{different subset of runs for each method. A }\DIFaddend detector that misses the hard events is scored only on the easy ones\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{and therefore }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, and so }\DIFaddend looks fast. \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Since }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Because }\DIFaddend the methods compared here differ \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{substantially
}\DIFdelend in miss rate, we also report a \DIFdelbegin \emph{\DIFdel{censored}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{delay}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{censored delay, }\DIFaddend in which a run that never alarms is charged the whole remaining horizon\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{rather than dropped}\DIFdelend . The two can rank methods \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{oppositely --- in }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{in opposite order. In }\DIFaddend \cref{sec:episodes} the changepoint mixture has the shorter conditional delay and the longer censored delay\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- and where they disagree}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. Where they disagree, }\DIFaddend the censored figure is the comparable one, \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{so }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{and }\DIFaddend head-to-head claims in this paper rest on it.
\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{This is not specific to our setting and we suspect it
affects published comparisons elsewhere.
}\DIFdelend 

Every comparison \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{is made at }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{uses }\DIFaddend a method's \emph{realised} $\PFA$, \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{never its nominal one, since a method whose nominal levelis meaningless }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{not its nominal level. A method that misreports its own error rate }\DIFaddend would otherwise appear to dominate\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{by misreporting its own error rate}\DIFdelend .

\paragraph{\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{From clips to streams}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Real-video protocol}\DIFaddend .}
\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{A clip-labelled corpus such as Mivia-IWDD becomes a streaming benchmark without
new data collection. Reserve a disjoint subset of confirmed-negative clips as
$\cD$; concatenate the remaining negative clips into a background stream of the
desired length; splice positive clips in at random onsets. Holding the
calibration clips out preserves the exchangeability the conformal layer needs. This protocol is implemented }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Each real video is monitored as its own stream, with a fresh detector. Calibration uses training videos only. The betting lag and every baseline knob are selected by leave-one-video-out on training videos, so no test frame informs an operating point (\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:real-data}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
). The splicing utility }\texttt{\DIFadd{build\_stream\_from\_clips}} \DIFadd{remains }\DIFaddend in the library \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{(}\texttt{\DIFdel{build\_stream\_from\_clips}}%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{) and is how \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:real-data}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
's Ped2
and Avenue streams are built once per-frame backbone scores for a real corpus
are in hand}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{for constructing long synthetic streams from clips}\DIFaddend .

\subsection{Layer 1: conformal calibration}
\label{sec:layer1}

\paragraph{Betting rate.}
The e-process of \cref{sec:episodic} \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{requires }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{needs }\DIFaddend increments that are conditionally super-uniform given the past. Video violates this badly\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{: at $25$\,fps the lag-one autocorrelation of backbone scores exceeds $0.85$ in our streams. We
estimate a decorrelation lag $\ell$ }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. Four lags are in play, and they are distinct:
}\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{\DIFadd{Pooled autocorrelation length.}} \DIFadd{On concatenated training videos the autocorrelation stays above $0.05$ beyond 200 lags. This reflects differences between video means, so we do not use it.
}\item \emph{\DIFadd{Within-video autocorrelation.}} \DIFadd{At lag one it is $0.82$--$0.97$ on the real backbones (\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:real-data}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
) and $\DiagAcfRaw$ in simulation.
}\item \emph{\DIFadd{Decorrelation lag $\ell$.}} \DIFadd{In simulation it is estimated }\DIFaddend from the calibration set alone\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{: $\ell = \AblLag$.
}\item \emph{\DIFadd{Betting lag $L^\ast$.}} \DIFadd{On real footage it is chosen by leave-one-video-out on training videos, because short, heterogeneous videos make an autocorrelation estimate unreliable.
}\end{itemize}
\DIFadd{For the simulated streams, $\ell$ is estimated after }\DIFaddend projecting out the context-predictable drift \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{first }\DIFdelend with a quadratic least-squares fit, since \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{otherwise }\DIFdelend the slow diurnal component \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{dominates the autocorrelation and returns }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{would otherwise return }\DIFaddend a uselessly large $\ell$\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- and }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. We }\DIFaddend place one bet per $\ell$ frames. \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{In our streams
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{With }\DIFaddend $\ell = \AblLag$, \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{so }\DIFdelend bets occur more than once per second, far finer than \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{the
timescale of }\DIFdelend a dumping event. \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{The same reasoning applies to the calibration
frames , which }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Calibration frames }\DIFaddend are consecutive video too\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{: }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, so }\DIFaddend they are thinned by \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{$\ell$ before use,
so the honest }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{the same lag. The effective }\DIFaddend calibration size is $n/\ell$ \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{and it is that number }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{(or $n/L^\ast$ on real footage), and }\DIFaddend the guarantee is stated in \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{that number}\DIFaddend . \Cref{sec:ablation} shows \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{this is not a refinement: }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{that }\DIFaddend betting on every frame is the \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{single largest }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{largest single }\DIFaddend source of invalidity we measured.

\paragraph{Conditional calibration.}
Let $S$ be a nonconformity score. The split-conformal p-value of a deployment frame is $p = (1 + |\{i : S_i \ge S\}|)/(n+1)$, which is super-uniform under exchangeability. \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Exchangeability between a }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{A }\DIFaddend night-time frame \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{and }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{is not exchangeable with }\DIFaddend a daylight-dominated $\cD$\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{is exactly what fails. We therefore conformalise the }\emph{\DIFdel{context-normalised residual}}
%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, so we conformalise the context-normalised residual
}\DIFaddend \begin{equation}
\label{eq:residual}
  z \;=\; \frac{s - \hat g(c)}{\hat\sigma(c)},
  \qquad
  \hat g(c) \approx \mathbb{E}[s \mid c],
  \quad
  \hat\sigma(c) \approx \mathbb{E}\bigl[|s - \hat g(c)| \,\big|\, c\bigr]\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{,
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{.
}\DIFaddend \end{equation}
\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{with }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Here }\DIFaddend $\hat g$ and $\hat\sigma$ \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{are }\DIFaddend ridge regressions on a quadratic-with-interactions basis in $c$, fitted on one half of $\cD$ and conformalised on the other. The split \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{matters: it }\DIFdelend makes the conformalisation residuals independent of the fitted models, so \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{conditionally on those models \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{thm:beta} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
applies verbatim. What
remains assumed }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{thm:beta} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
applies conditionally on them. The remaining assumption }\DIFaddend is that the normalised residual \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{in \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{eq:residual} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
is distributed independently }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{is independent }\DIFaddend of $c$\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- exchangeability is bought by the
normalisation , not by the split --- and \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:limitations}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
returns to what
happens when the model is wrong. The gain over binning is that the whole held-out
half backs every context value, rather than one bin's worth backing each. We implement Mondrian calibration as well}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. The normalisation provides this, not the split (\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:limitations}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
). Mondrian calibration is also implemented}\DIFaddend , since it is exactly group-conditional\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{and preferable when a
single context variable dominates; }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. }\DIFaddend \cref{sec:shift} shows where each wins.

Because a regression can be extrapolated but a guarantee cannot, frames whose context falls outside the region \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{covered by }\DIFaddend $\cD$ \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{covers --- a per-coordinate box widened by a margin, intersected with a Mahalanobis check for jointly novel combinations ---
}\DIFdelend are reported inactive, and the detector bets neutrally on them. \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{A regime that was
never calibrated cannot be certified, and the }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{The }\DIFaddend abstention rate reports how often \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{that happensrather than hiding it}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{this happens. Under shift the recommended safeguard is the stricter support region, with the Mahalanobis slack set to 0.5 instead of the default 1.5, and the abstention rate is the monitor that signals recalibration (\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:shift}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
)}\DIFaddend .

\paragraph{Calibration-conditional validity.}
The same finite $\cD$ scores every frame for months, so the \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{relevant statement is
conditional }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{guarantee must hold conditionally }\DIFaddend on $\cD$\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, and conditionally }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. Conditionally, }\DIFaddend the p-values deviate from uniform by $O(n^{-1/2})$. The standard remedy adds the Dvoretzky--Kiefer--Wolfowitz deviation $\varepsilon_n(\delta) = \sqrt{\log(2/\delta)/(2n)}$ \citep{dkw1956,massart1990} to every p-value. \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{It works and it is far too blunt:
at }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{At }\DIFaddend $n=\CalN{}$, $\delta=\CalDelta{}$ \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{it }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{this }\DIFaddend moves the smallest attainable p-value from $\CalRawPmin{}$ to $\DkwPmin{}$, \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{capping the evidence a single frame can
supply at well under one nat. The tail is the only part a }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{which is too blunt. A }\DIFaddend betting detector uses \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{.
}%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{We }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{only the tail, so we }\DIFaddend use the exact distribution instead.

\begin{theorem}[Exact calibration-conditional levels]
\label{thm:beta}
Let $S_1,\dots,S_n$ be i.i.d.\ from a continuous $F$ and let $S$ be an
independent draw from $F$. For $j = 0,\dots,n-1$ let $\delta_j > 0$ with
$\sum_j \delta_j \le \delta$, and set
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:betalevels}
  q_j \;=\; \mathrm{Beta}^{-1}\bigl(1-\delta_j;\; j+1,\; n-j\bigr),
  \qquad q_n = 1 .
\end{equation}
Define $\tilde p = q_{J}$ with $J = |\{i : S_i \ge S\}|$. Then there is an event
$E$ with $\mathbb{P}(E) \ge 1-\delta$, measurable with respect to
$S_1,\dots,S_n$, on which
$\mathbb{P}\bigl(\tilde p \le u \mid S_1,\dots,S_n\bigr) \le u$
for every $u \in [0,1]$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Conditionally on the calibration sample, $\{J \le j\} = \{S > S_{(n-j)}\}$ where
$S_{(1)} \le \cdots \le S_{(n)}$ are the order statistics, so
$\mathbb{P}(J \le j \mid \cD) = 1 - F(S_{(n-j)})$. Since $F$ is continuous,
$F(S_{(k)}) \sim \mathrm{Beta}(k, n+1-k)$, hence
$1 - F(S_{(n-j)}) \sim \mathrm{Beta}(j+1, n-j)$. By \cref{eq:betalevels},
$\mathbb{P}\bigl(1 - F(S_{(n-j)}) > q_j\bigr) = \delta_j$. Let $E$ be the
intersection over $j$ of the complements; a union bound gives
$\mathbb{P}(E) \ge 1 - \sum_j \delta_j \ge 1-\delta$. On $E$,
$\mathbb{P}(\tilde p \le q_j \mid \cD) = \mathbb{P}(J \le j \mid \cD) \le q_j$
for every $j$, and since $\tilde p$ takes values only in $\{q_0,\dots,q_n\}$
and $q_j$ is non-decreasing in $j$, super-uniformity at the atoms extends to all
$u$.
\end{proof}

We spend the budget as $\delta_j \propto (j+1)^{-2}$, concentrating confidence where the betting functions look\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{; under }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. Under }\DIFaddend this allocation $\{q_j\}$ is verified non-decreasing in $j$, which \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{is what }\DIFdelend identifies $\{\tilde p \le q_j\} = \{J \le j\}$ in the proof above\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{(the }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. The }\DIFaddend implementation takes a running maximum as a safeguard \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{against an allocation for which this fails)}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{for any allocation where this fails}\DIFaddend . At $n=\CalN{}$, $\delta=\CalDelta{}$ this gives $q_0 = \BetaPmin{}$\DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, }\DIFaddend against DKW's $\DkwPmin{}$. \Cref{thm:beta} assumes i.i.d.\ calibration draws, which is why the calibration set is thinned\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{; \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:ablation} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
does not resolve whether skipping the
thinning costs validity at the replication count run there (\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:limitations}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
), though the calibration-conditional argument no longer applies once consecutive
draws are dependent}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. At 1,500 null streams, unthinned calibration stayed valid (\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:ablation}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
), so the experiments do not show that thinning is needed in practice}\DIFaddend .

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Finally, when }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{When }\DIFaddend deployment reweights context that $\cD$ \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{does cover}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{covers}\DIFaddend , weighted conformal calibration \citep{tibshirani2019covariate} applies. We \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{implement it
with }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{use }\DIFaddend a logistic density-ratio estimate, \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{gate it behind a held-out
classifier }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{gated behind a }\DIFaddend two-sample test\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{so it engages only when a shift is detectable, and take the calibration-conditional correction at }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, and replace $n$ in \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{eq:betalevels} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
by }\DIFaddend the Kish effective sample size \citep{kish1965}\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{in place of $n$ in \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{eq:betalevels}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
. Unlike \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{thm:beta}
}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
itself, this substitution is not accompanied by a proof: \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{thm:beta} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
's
order-statistic argument }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. This substitution has no calibration-conditional proof, because \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{thm:beta} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
}\DIFaddend assumes equal weights\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, and the Kish size is an
approximation to the effective count under unequal weights that is exact only
in that equal-weight limit, not a derived calibration-conditional bound for the
weighted case. \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\Cref{sec:shift} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
reports that in our setting the residual
construction of \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{eq:residual} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
dominates it on power, which we take to be a
fact about the setting rather than about the technique: reweighting corrects
the marginal context distribution, while a sequential test is broken by
}\emph{\DIFdel{conditional}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{miscalibration; \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:limitations} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
returns to what the
weighted variant's validity evidence does and does not show}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. The weighted path is therefore a heuristic, evaluated empirically in \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:shift}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
, and it is not the recommended safeguard}\DIFaddend .

\subsection{Layer 2: the episodic e-process}
\label{sec:episodic}

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{This is the core of the method. }\DIFdelend Let $p_1, p_2, \ldots$ be the p-values at the betting instants. A \emph{betting function} is a non-increasing $f : (0,1] \to [0,\infty)$ with $\int_0^1 f \le 1$\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{; then
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. Then }\DIFaddend $\mathbb{E}_{H_0}[f(p)] \le 1$ for any super-uniform $p$, so $f(p)$ is an e-value. We use the power family $f_\kappa(p) = \kappa p^{\kappa-1}$, $\kappa \in (0,1]$, with $\kappa = 1$ the neutral bet $f \equiv 1$.

\subsubsection{Mixing over episodes}

Let $z_t \in \{\text{quiet}, \text{active}\}$ be a two-state Markov chain with onset probability $\mathbb{P}(\text{quiet} \to \text{active}) = \rho$ and \emph{end} probability $\mathbb{P}(\text{active} \to \text{quiet}) = \eta$, started quiet\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, and let the wealth mix }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. The wealth mixes }\DIFaddend over every trajectory of that chain:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:episodic}
  W_t \;=\; \sum_{z_{1:t}} \mathbb{P}(z_{1:t})
            \prod_{s \le t} f(p_s)^{\ind{z_s = \text{active}}} .
\end{equation}
Each trajectory contributes the product of betting factors over the frames it declares active, weighted by its prior probability. The classical \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{mixture over
changepoints }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{changepoint mixture }\DIFaddend is the special case in which the chain, once active, \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{can never return: it mixes over }\emph{\DIFdel{when}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{never returns. It mixes over when }\DIFaddend a change starts\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, whereas \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{eq:episodic}
}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
mixes over }\emph{\DIFdel{when it starts and when it stops}}%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\Cref{eq:episodic} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
mixes over when episodes start and stop}\DIFaddend , and over how many \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{times that
happens}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{occur}\DIFaddend .

The sum has $2^t$ terms and collapses to two numbers. Writing $A_t$ and $Q_t$ for the parts of the sum whose trajectories end active and quiet respectively,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:forward}
  A_t = \bigl[(1-\eta)A_{t-1} + \rho\,Q_{t-1}\bigr] f(p_t),
  \qquad
  Q_t = \eta\,A_{t-1} + (1-\rho)\,Q_{t-1},
\end{equation}
with $A_0 = 0$, $Q_0 = 1$ and $W_t = A_t + Q_t$. This is the forward recursion of a hidden Markov model\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, and it is }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. It costs }\DIFaddend $O(1)$ per frame in \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{both }\DIFdelend time and memory.

\begin{theorem}[Time-uniform validity of the episodic e-process]
\label{thm:episodic}
Under $\Pinf$, if the $p_t$ are conditionally super-uniform given
$\Fcal_{t-1}$, then $(W_t)$ defined by \cref{eq:episodic} is a non-negative
supermartingale with $W_0 = 1$, and for any $\alpha \in (0,1)$
\begin{equation*}
  \Pinf\bigl(\exists\, t \ge 1 : W_t \ge 1/\alpha\bigr) \;\le\; \alpha .
\end{equation*}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Non-negativity is immediate and $W_0 = Q_0 = 1$. From \cref{eq:forward},
$A_{t}$ carries exactly one factor $f(p_t)$ and $Q_t$ carries none, so
\begin{align*}
  \mathbb{E}[W_t \mid \Fcal_{t-1}]
   &= \bigl[(1-\eta)A_{t-1} + \rho Q_{t-1}\bigr]\,
      \mathbb{E}[f(p_t) \mid \Fcal_{t-1}]
      \;+\; \eta A_{t-1} + (1-\rho) Q_{t-1} \\
   &\le (1-\eta)A_{t-1} + \rho Q_{t-1} + \eta A_{t-1} + (1-\rho) Q_{t-1}
    \;=\; A_{t-1} + Q_{t-1} \;=\; W_{t-1},
\end{align*}
using $\mathbb{E}[f(p_t) \mid \Fcal_{t-1}] \le 1$ and the fact that the
transition probabilities out of each state sum to one. Ville's inequality
\citep{ville1939} applied to the non-negative supermartingale $(W_t)$ started at
one gives the bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[End-to-end level]
\label{cor:composition}
Let $E$ be the calibration event of \cref{thm:beta}, with
$\mathbb{P}(E) \ge 1 - \delta$, on which the deployment p-values $\tilde p_t$
are conditionally super-uniform given $\Fcal_{t-1}$. Then the episodic
e-process run on $(\tilde p_t)$ satisfies
$\Pinf(\exists\, t \ge 1 : W_t \ge 1/\alpha) \le \alpha + \delta$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
On $E$, \cref{thm:episodic} applies verbatim and gives probability at most
$\alpha$ conditional on $\cD$; off $E$, the probability is bounded by $1$, and
$\mathbb{P}(E^c) \le \delta$. Removing the conditioning,
$\Pinf(\exists t : W_t \ge 1/\alpha) \le \mathbb{P}(E)\cdot\alpha +
\mathbb{P}(E^c)\cdot 1 \le \alpha + \delta$.
\end{proof}
\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{This is the guarantee actually delivered end to end, and it is what }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{The end-to-end level is therefore $\alpha + \delta$, as }\DIFaddend the library documents \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{(}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{in }\DIFaddend \texttt{sentinel\_e/pipeline.py}\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{) even where earlier drafts of this
text quoted $\alpha$ alone}\DIFdelend . With the default $\delta = 10^{-3}$\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{this is a
$10$--$20\%$ relative loosening at the operating points \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:validity}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
reports ($\alpha \in \{0.005,\dots,0.5\}$), not a rounding matter, and every
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, the relative loosening is $20\%$ at the smallest level in \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:validity}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
, $\alpha = 0.005$, and it falls as $\alpha$ grows. Every }\DIFaddend level plotted in this paper should be read as $\alpha + \delta$.

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Two things are worth drawing out. First, the episode structure is }\emph{\DIFdel{free}}%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{:
it needed no new machinery and cost }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{The episode structure costs }\DIFaddend nothing in the guarantee, because the transition probabilities telescope exactly. \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Second, the recursion never grows. Evaluated
term by term, a }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{A term-by-term }\DIFaddend mixture over candidate starts \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{carries one term per start, at
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{costs }\DIFaddend $O(t)$ work per observation \citep{saha2026nonpartitioned}\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{; \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{eq:forward} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
carries }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, whereas \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{eq:forward} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
keeps }\DIFaddend two numbers per grid point\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{whatever $t$ is}\DIFdelend . The geometric-prior changepoint mixture also \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{telescopes }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{reduces }\DIFaddend to $O(1)$ (\cref{prop:generalisation})\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, so
the honest statement is not that we are cheaper than }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. The gain is therefore not speed over }\DIFaddend that special case\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{but that
we match it while spanning }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, but }\DIFaddend episodes of finite duration \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- month-long monitoring
on edge hardware stays arithmetically trivial }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{at the same cost }\DIFaddend (\cref{sec:compute}).

\begin{proposition}[Strict generalisation]
\label{prop:generalisation}
With $\eta = 0$ the recursion \cref{eq:forward} coincides with the mixture over
changepoint locations under a geometric prior with hazard $\rho$\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{; discarding
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, with the
unspent tail mass retained. Discarding }\DIFaddend $Q_t$ \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{from that recovers in turn }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{yields a statistic that is not a
supermartingale and that tends to }\DIFaddend the classical Shiryaev--Roberts statistic
\DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{only in the limit $\rho \to 0$ after rescaling}\DIFaddend .
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
With $\eta = 0$, $Q_t = (1-\rho)Q_{t-1}$ so $Q_t = (1-\rho)^t$ is the prior mass
on ``no episode has begun by $t$'', and
$A_t = (A_{t-1} + \rho Q_{t-1}) f(p_t)$ with $\rho Q_{t-1} = w_t$ the geometric
prior mass on a change at $t$. Unrolling gives
$A_t = \sum_{j \le t} w_j \prod_{s=j}^{t} f(p_s)$, whence
$W_t = \sum_{j\le t} w_j \prod_{s=j}^t f(p_s) + \sum_{j>t} w_j$, Shiryaev's
Bayesian statistic under the geometric prior with hazard $\rho$
\citep{shiryaev1963}. Dropping $Q_t$ leaves $\sum_{j\le t} w_j \prod_{s=j}^t
