f(p_s)$ alone\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, which }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. This }\DIFaddend is not a supermartingale\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{: }\DIFaddend the telescoping in
\cref{thm:episodic} fails by exactly $w_t$ per step\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- and, taking }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. Taking }\DIFaddend the
$\rho \to 0$ limit under the appropriate rescaling \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, }\DIFdelend recovers the classical
Shiryaev--Roberts sum with unit start weights \citep{roberts1966}\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{; at fixed
$\rho > 0$ the two named statistics remain distinct objects , and only the
proof's algebraic point --- that discarding the quiet mass breaks the martingale property --- depends on neither.
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{.
}\DIFaddend \end{proof}

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{So }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{The statement thus separates two objects that are easy to conflate: the changepoint mixture, which }\DIFaddend the episodic process \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{sits strictly above both, and the axis it adds ---
episode duration --- is one this anytime-valid e-process family has held
fixed at infinity; \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:related} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
positions that claim against the
classical transient- and intermittent-change literature, which has not. The
empirical consequence is in \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:episodes}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
: where the classical model is
right, the two coincide within the noise of a paired comparison; where events
recur, a gap opens whose size that same section resolves with uncertainty
rather than by a single point estimate}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{contains exactly at $\eta=0$, and Shiryaev--Roberts, which differs from it at any fixed $\rho>0$}\DIFaddend .

\subsubsection{Intermittency \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, }\DIFdelend by dilation \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{(}\DIFaddend a negative result\DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{)}\DIFaddend }
\label{sec:dilation}

Within an episode only a fraction $\pi$ of frames show the act, which suggests \emph{dilating} the emission:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:dilated}
  f_\pi(p) \;=\; (1-\pi) + \pi\, f(p).
\end{equation}
This is \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{legal, and cheaply so: $f_\pi$ }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{a legal betting function. It }\DIFaddend is a convex combination of the neutral bet and $f$, hence non-increasing with $\int_0^1 f_\pi \le 1$, so \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{it is a betting
function and }\DIFdelend \cref{thm:episodic} applies verbatim. It \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{also says the right thing
--- }\emph{\DIFdel{this frame belongs to an episode, but it may or may not show the act}}%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{.
}%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{We expected this to be the answer to intermittency. It is not , and we report
that rather than quietly shipping it}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{does not help}\DIFaddend . Mixing over $\kappa$ already \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{handles
intermittency: a stream in which a third of the episode frames are anomalous is
best played with }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{covers intermittency, because }\DIFaddend a milder bet \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, and the $\kappa$ grid contains one. A $\pi$ axis
therefore buys no behaviour the grid did not already have, while spending prior
mass to do it}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{suits an episode with fewer anomalous frames}\DIFaddend . In the ablation, \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{enabling it }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{dilation }\DIFaddend lengthens censored detection delay from \AblFullDelay\,s to \AblDilationDelay\,s (\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{tab:ablation}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Online Resource~1, Table~S7}\DIFaddend ), and \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{we
found }\DIFdelend no intermittency level \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{at which it repaid that cost--- below
$\pi \approx 0.2$ both variants fail together, and above it the plain $\kappa$
mixture is ahead}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{repays that cost}\DIFaddend . The default grid therefore sets $\pi = 1$. \DIFdelbegin %DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{We keep \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{eq:dilated} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
in the exposition and the implementation for two
reasons. It is the natural thing to try, so a reader is owed the evidence rather
than left to repeat the experiment; and the construction remains available for a
backbone }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{The construction stays in the implementation for backbones }\DIFaddend whose anomalous frames are \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{far }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{much }\DIFaddend stronger relative to \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{its }\DIFdelend quiet ones than ours\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, where the trade-off could plausibly reverse. What it is not is a
contribution, and we do not count it as one}\DIFdelend .

\subsubsection{Staying parameter-free}
\label{sec:parameterfree}

Nothing above \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{requires knowing }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{needs }\DIFaddend $(\rho, \eta, \pi, \kappa)$. We place a prior grid over all four and run one recursion per grid point\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, mixing the results:
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. The mixture }\DIFaddend $W_t = \sum_g \lambda_g W_t^{(g)}$ with $\sum_g \lambda_g = 1$ is a convex combination of supermartingales\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{and }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, }\DIFaddend hence a supermartingale. The deployed grid is \EpiGridKappa{} values of $\kappa$ by \EpiGridEta{} of $\eta$ by \EpiGridPi{} of $\pi$ (dilation off by default\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:dilation}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
), i.e.\
\EpiGridPoints{} points, so the per-frame }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{), giving \EpiGridPoints{} points. The }\DIFaddend cost is a \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{few dozen floating-point
operations and still $O(1)$ in $t$; enabling the $\pi$ grid (\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:ablation}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
)
triples the pointcount and the cost with it}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{constant number of operations per grid point}\DIFaddend , still $O(1)$ \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{. In practice the
mixture is what makes the method usable: an }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{in $t$. An }\DIFaddend operator does not know how long a dumping event lasts\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{at their site}\DIFdelend , and the grid spans everything from a ten-second burst to a permanent change.

\subsubsection{The episode posterior}

The recursion \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{also }\DIFaddend yields, at no extra cost, the posterior probability that the camera is inside an episode \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{right }\DIFdelend now,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:posterior}
  \Pi_t \;=\; \frac{A_t}{A_t + Q_t} \;\in\; [0, 1],
\end{equation}
prior-averaged over the grid. \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{This is a more useful thing to look at, and to
transmit, than accumulated wealth: it is bounded , it is }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{It is bounded and }\DIFaddend interpretable as a probability, and it is comparable between a camera installed last week and one that has \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{been running }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{run }\DIFaddend for a month. \Cref{sec:layer3} uses it as the message the camera graph exchanges.

\subsubsection{Numerics}

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Carrying \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{eq:forward} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Working }\DIFaddend in log space would need two \texttt{logaddexp} calls per grid point per frame. Instead we renormalise $(A_t, Q_t)$ to sum to one at \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{every }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{each }\DIFaddend step and accumulate the \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{logarithm }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{log }\DIFaddend of the scale factor, \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{which is }\DIFdelend the standard HMM scaling trick\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{: the }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. The }\DIFaddend two-state simplex cannot underflow\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, $\log W_t$
is the accumulated log-scale, and the per-step work is a handful of vector
operations on the grid. \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\Cref{alg:episodic} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
states }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\Cref{alg:episodic} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
gives }\DIFaddend the per-frame update\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{in
full; it is a direct transcription of }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, which transcribes the inner loop of }\DIFaddend \texttt{sentinel\_e/episodic.py}\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{'s inner loop , not a simplification of it}\DIFdelend .

\begin{algorithm}[t]
\caption{One frame of the episodic e-process, vectorised over the prior grid}
\label{alg:episodic}
\begin{algorithmic}[1]
\Require prior grid $\{(\kappa_g, \eta_g, \pi_g)\}_{g=1}^G$ with log-prior
  weights $\log\lambda_g$, $\sum_g \lambda_g = 1$; onset hazard $\rho$ (or
  $\rho_t$, if predictable modulation is enabled, \cref{thm:predictable});
  alarm threshold $1/\alpha$
\State $A_g \gets 0,\; Q_g \gets 1,\; s_g \gets 0 \;\; \forall g$
  \Comment{$s_g$: per-grid-point log-scale; $A_g + Q_g = 1$ is the invariant}
\For{each betting instant $t = 1, 2, \ldots$}
  \State receive conformal p-value $p_t$ (or skip this $t$: thinning / abstention)
  \For{each grid point $g$ \textbf{(vectorised, not a real loop)}}
    \State $f_g \gets (1 - \pi_g) + \pi_g\, \kappa_g\, p_t^{\,\kappa_g - 1}$
      \Comment{dilated power bet, \cref{eq:dilated}; $\pi_g = 1$ if dilation off}
    \State $A_g' \gets \bigl[(1-\eta_g)\, A_g + \rho\, Q_g\bigr] f_g$
    \State $Q_g' \gets \eta_g\, A_g + (1-\rho)\, Q_g$
      \Comment{\cref{eq:forward}, one grid point}
    \State $c_g \gets A_g' + Q_g'$
      \Comment{restores the invariant; $c_g > 0$ always}
    \State $A_g \gets A_g' / c_g,\;\; Q_g \gets Q_g' / c_g$
      \Comment{HMM scaling trick: the two-state simplex cannot underflow}
    \State $s_g \gets s_g + \log c_g$
  \EndFor
  \State $\log W_t \gets \operatorname{logsumexp}_g\bigl(\log\lambda_g + s_g\bigr)$
    \Comment{mixture happens here, in log space, not by renormalising a joint sum}
  \State $\Pi_t \gets \sum_g \lambda_g\, e^{s_g} A_g \,\big/\, e^{\log W_t}$
    \Comment{episode posterior, \cref{eq:posterior}}
  \If{$\log W_t \ge \log(1/\alpha)$}
    \State \textbf{alarm}; if restarting, reset $A_g \gets 0,\, Q_g \gets 1,\,
      s_g \gets 0\;\; \forall g$
  \EndIf
\EndFor
\end{algorithmic}
\end{algorithm}

Two \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{things }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{points }\DIFaddend about \cref{alg:episodic} are \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{worth making explicit because they are
}\DIFdelend easy to miss \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{on a first read of }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{from }\DIFaddend \cref{eq:forward} alone. \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{First, every }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Each }\DIFaddend grid point keeps its \DIFdelbegin \emph{\DIFdel{own}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{running }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{own }\DIFaddend invariant $A_g + Q_g = 1$ and \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{its own
}\DIFdelend log-scale $s_g$\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{; the mixture over the grid is not a joint renormalisation but }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. The mixture is }\DIFaddend a single \texttt{logsumexp}\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{taken }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, evaluated }\DIFaddend only when $\log W_t$ or $\Pi_t$ is read out, so adding grid points \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{costs a longer vector , not a different algorithm. Second,
the inner loop over $g$ is the only place the grid appears and it is fully
vectorised in the implementation (one array operation per line, not a Python
loop), so nothing about the recursion's cost depends on how the grid was
chosen: the parameter-free stance of \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:parameterfree} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
costs exactly
$|\{g\}|$ times the single-hypothesis cost, not more}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{lengthens a vector without changing the algorithm}\DIFaddend . The normalising division \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{(line 8) }\DIFdelend is exact, \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{not a truncation or a clip, so no approximation enters
between \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{eq:forward} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
and what the code computes beyond ordinary
floating-point rounding , which \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:validity}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
's oracle-p-value arm }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{and the only error beyond rounding is what the oracle p-value arm of \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:validity} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
}\DIFaddend is designed to expose\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{if it mattered}\DIFdelend .

\paragraph{A worked numeric trace.}
\Cref{tab:worked-recursion} runs four steps of \cref{alg:episodic} \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{by hand, }\DIFdelend at a single grid point ($\kappa = 0.3$, $\eta = 0.1$, $\rho = 0.01$, dilation off)\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{so that every entry is checkable }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, so every entry can be checked }\DIFaddend with a calculator\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{: $f_t = \kappa\,
p_t^{\kappa - 1}$, $A_t' = [(1-\eta)A_{t-1} + \rho\, Q_{t-1}]\, f_t$, $Q_t' =
\eta\, A_{t-1} + (1-\rho)\, Q_{t-1}$, }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. Here }\DIFaddend $c_t = A_t' + Q_t'$ \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, and the running
wealth is }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{and }\DIFaddend $W_t = \exp(\sum_{s\le t} \log c_s)$.

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Three things this trace makes concrete that the general statement can leave
abstract. First, a single }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{A }\DIFaddend small p-value \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{(}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{at }\DIFaddend $t=1$ \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, $p_1=0.02$, a moderately
surprising frame) moves }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{($p_1 = 0.02$) multiplies }\DIFaddend $A_t$ \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{from $0$ to $0.045$ in one step but moves $W_t$
by less than }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{by $f_1 \approx 4.64$, yet $W_1$ rises by under }\DIFaddend $4\%$\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- the betting factor $f_1 = 4.64$ is diluted by }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, because }\DIFaddend the prior mass $Q_0 = 1$ \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{still sitting }\DIFdelend on ``no episode yet'' \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, which is the entire content
of mixing over onset times rather than betting on frame one being the change. Second}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{dilutes the bet. At $t=3$}\DIFaddend , $W_t$ \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{genuinely falls below $1$ at $t=3$ ($p_3 = 0.9$, an unsurprising
frame under the null): this is the supermartingale property in miniature ---
wealth is not required to }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{falls below one. Wealth need not }\DIFaddend grow, only \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{to }\DIFdelend have conditional expectation at most its previous value\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- and it is why a single early ``lucky'' small p-value
cannot, by itself, manufacture a violation of \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{thm:episodic}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
. Third,
$A_t \ll Q_t$ throughout this short trace, because four frames is not enough
evidence at this $\kappa$ to move the posterior far from ``quiet'' even though
one of the four p-values was small; \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{eq:posterior}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
's $\Pi_t = A_t$ here
(the single-grid-point case), so this trace's }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. Over four frames $A_t \ll Q_t$, so }\DIFaddend $\Pi_4 \approx 0.049$ \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{is a small
number for an honest reason}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{reflects too little evidence}\DIFaddend , not a \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{sign that anything failed}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{fault}\DIFaddend .

\subsection{Layer 3: the camera network}
\label{sec:layer3}

\paragraph{A predictable spatial prior.}
Offenders return to the same lay-by and, when disturbed, move a few hundred metres and dump again\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, so an }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. An }\DIFaddend episode at one camera is \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{genuine }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{therefore }\DIFaddend prior information about its neighbours. We build a graph over the fleet\DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, }\DIFaddend with edge weights combining spatial proximity and static-context similarity\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, and a }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. A }\DIFaddend two-layer message-passing network maps each camera's features to an onset hazard $\rho_{i,t}$ and a stake scale $m_{i,t}$.

The features \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{are where }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{use }\DIFaddend the episode posterior of \cref{eq:posterior}\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{earns its
place. A camera's }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. Raw }\DIFaddend log-wealth grows without bound and depends on \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{how long it has
been running, so aggregating raw wealth over a neighbourhood lets one
long-installed camera dominate its neighbours regardless of what is happening
there}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{installation time, so a neighbourhood average of it would be dominated by the oldest camera}\DIFaddend . $\Pi_{i,t}$ is bounded \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{in $[0,1]$ and answers a question that means the same thing }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{and answers the same question }\DIFaddend at every camera\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- }\emph{\DIFdel{is there an episode here now?}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{--- so a mean
over neighbours }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, so a neighbourhood mean }\DIFaddend is meaningful. The network sees $\Pi_{i,t-1}$, the normalised log-wealth, three exponential moving averages of $-\log p$, static context and degree, all measurable at $t-1$.

Because the network passes messages, \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{camera $i$'s inputs at $t$ --- and hence
}\DIFdelend $\rho_{i,t}$ and $m_{i,t}$ \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- depend on neighbours' features and so on }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{depend on }\DIFaddend the \emph{fleet's} joint history\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, not camera $i$'s own}\DIFdelend . Write $\Gfilt_{t-1} = \sigma(p_{j,s} : j \in \text{fleet},\, s \le t-1)$ for that \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{joint filtration;
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{filtration. Then }\DIFaddend $\Fcal_{t-1} \subseteq \Gfilt_{t-1}$ is camera $i$'s own\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{. The }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, and the }\DIFaddend theorem below is stated \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, and needed, }\DIFdelend at the joint level.

\begin{theorem}[Learned control cannot break the guarantee]
\label{thm:predictable}
Suppose $\rho_{i,t} \in [0,1]$ and $m_{i,t} \in [0,1]$ are
$\Gfilt_{t-1}$-measurable, the map $(m,\kappa) \mapsto f_{\pi,\kappa_{\mathrm{eff}}}$
with $\kappa_{\mathrm{eff}} = 1-m(1-\kappa)$ is jointly measurable, and $p_{i,t}$
is conditionally super-uniform given $\Gfilt_{t-1}$ (the joint history, not
merely camera $i$'s own). Then \cref{thm:episodic} holds verbatim for every
camera, for any such choice of $\rho_{i,t}, m_{i,t}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For $m_{i,t} \in [0,1]$ and $\kappa \in (0,1]$ we have
$\kappa_{\mathrm{eff}} \in (0,1]$, so $f_{\kappa_{\mathrm{eff}}}$ is a betting
function and by \cref{eq:dilated} so is its dilation, for every fixed value of
$\kappa_{\mathrm{eff}}$. Since $(m_{i,t}, \pi)$ is $\Gfilt_{t-1}$-measurable and
the map to $f_{\pi,\kappa_{\mathrm{eff}}}$ is jointly measurable in its
argument and in $p_{i,t}$, conditioning on $\Gfilt_{t-1}$ freezes
$\kappa_{\mathrm{eff}}$ to its realised value and
$\mathbb{E}[f_{\pi,\kappa_{\mathrm{eff}}}(p_{i,t}) \mid \Gfilt_{t-1}] \le 1$
follows from conditional super-uniformity at that fixed value\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- this }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. This }\DIFaddend is the
standard freezing argument for a regular conditional distribution, not a
step that treats $f_{\kappa_{\mathrm{eff}}}$ as linear in $\kappa_{\mathrm{eff}}$.
Replacing $\rho$ by $\rho_{i,t}$ in \cref{eq:forward} leaves the transition
probabilities out of each state summing to one, which is all the telescoping in
\cref{thm:episodic} uses, now run against $\Gfilt_{t-1}$ throughout. The
supermartingale step is therefore unchanged.
\end{proof}

The hypothesis that matters in practice is \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{the }\DIFdelend conditional super-uniformity \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{of
$p_{i,t}$ }\DIFdelend given the \emph{joint} fleet history\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, which is strictly stronger than what }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. This is stronger than }\DIFaddend \cref{sec:layer1} establishes for \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{a single camerain isolation: }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{one camera, because }\DIFaddend the message-passing layer \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{is built to couple }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{couples }\DIFaddend cameras that share weather, lighting and traffic\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, which is exactly the kind of cross-camera dependence that could in
principle carry information about $p_{i,t}$ beyond camera $i$'s own past}\DIFdelend . \Cref{sec:diagnostics} does not certify \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{this stronger condition; itis assumed
here, and the honest scope of \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{thm:predictable} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{it, so the theorem }\DIFaddend is conditional on it\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{holding, with e-BH's arbitrary-dependence guarantee at the combination step
providing no help at the level of a single camera's e-value. What the theorem
still delivers unconditionally is narrower but real: whatever
$\rho_{i,t}, m_{i,t}$ the network outputs , it enters only through a predictable multiplicative }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. Unconditionally, the learned outputs enter only as a predictable }\DIFaddend reweighting of a supermartingale step\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{that already holds or
already fails on its own terms, so a badly-behaved learned component cannot
be the }\emph{\DIFdel{cause}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{of }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. A badly behaved component therefore cannot cause }\DIFaddend an invalid step that a well-behaved one would not also \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{have}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{cause}\DIFaddend . \Cref{tab:network}'s all-null columns check the practical consequence\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{directly, since a theorem about the idealised object is not by itself evidence
about the implementation}\DIFdelend .

Training is offline on held-out simulated fleets\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, maximising }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. It maximises }\DIFaddend post-change log-wealth growth\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, }\DIFaddend the Kelly/GROW criterion \citep{kelly1956}, \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{the standard
proxy for minimising }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{with a penalty on wealth burned at unaffected cameras. Growth rate proxies }\DIFaddend delay, since \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{the delay is essentially }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{delay is roughly }\DIFaddend the time for \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{the
}\DIFdelend wealth to climb \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{from one }\DIFdelend to $1/\alpha$ at that rate\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- with a penalty on wealth
burned at unaffected cameras}\DIFdelend . The recursion is differentiable, so gradients reach both heads.

\paragraph{Fleet decisions.}
At a stopping time $\tau_i = \min(\text{alarm}, T)$, optional stopping gives $\Einf[W_{i,\tau_i}] \le 1$, so each camera's wealth is a valid e-value. We apply e-BH \citep{wang2022ebh}: sort $E_{(1)} \ge \cdots \ge E_{(K)}$ and reject the top $k^\star = \max\{k : E_{(k)} \ge K/(\alpha k)\}$. This controls FDR at $\alpha$ under \emph{arbitrary} dependence, which \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{is what the setting demands: }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{the setting needs because }\DIFaddend adjacent cameras share weather, lighting and traffic\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, and Layer 3
couples them deliberately}\DIFdelend . BH on p-values would need positive regression dependence or a Benjamini--Yekutieli $\log K$ penalty \citep{benjamini2001}\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{; }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. }\DIFaddend e-BH needs neither. \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{A }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{The wealth is read at a stopping time, since a }\DIFaddend running maximum of the wealth would not be a valid e-value\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, which is why the reading is taken at a stopping
time}\DIFdelend .

\section{Experiments and results}
\label{sec:results}

\subsection{Protocol}
\label{sec:protocol}

\DIFdelbegin \paragraph{\DIFdel{Two evidence sources, used for different things.}}
%DIFAUXCMD
\addtocounter{paragraph}{-1}%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{This paper reports both simulated and real streams, and they }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Two sources of evidence }\DIFaddend answer different questions. \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\Cref{sec:real-data} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
is the
paper's empirical basis for what a real
backbone, on real surveillance footage, actually delivers: real per-frame
optical-flow scores from two public video-anomaly-detection corpora , real
train/test splits, real spliced anomalous events, and --- reported as a
finding rather than a footnote --- a real , severe autocorrelation structure
that leaves only a narrow band of usable operating points. It is also, by
construction, limited to the two datasets' native horizons (minutes, not
weeks) and
to the one backbone we computed scores with. The simulated streams
that follow exist to answer the question the real corpora cannot: what
happens at the $10^5$--$10^6$-frame horizons, the wide range of episode counts
and intermittency levels, and the calibration/deployment shift severities a
municipal deployment must survive, but that no released corpus --- Mivia-IWDD
included --- provides months of continuously-labelled per-frame detector
output to test directly. Neither source substitutes for the other, and \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:limitations} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
states plainly what each does and does not establish}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Simulated streams vary the
horizon, the episode structure and the calibration shift, and each run
reproduces from its seed. Real video (\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:real-data}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
) tests the guarantee
on public corpora with real detector scores, but each corpus is short and
covers one backbone at a time. On real video, the betting lag and every
baseline knob are chosen on training videos only, by leave-one-video-out. Each
test video is monitored alone, and no test frame is reused}\DIFaddend .

\paragraph{Simulated streams.}
SENTINEL-E consumes one scalar per frame, so the \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{faithful way to stress the
guarantee layer at the timescales that matter --- weeks of video, $10^6$ frames
--- is to model }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{simulator generates }\DIFaddend that
scalar. \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Our simulator is deliberately unfriendly in the ways
real footage is: a diurnal illumination }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{The null score has a diurnal }\DIFaddend cycle and a two-state weather chain\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{shift
the null score }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, and
both shift its }\DIFaddend distribution after calibration\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{; scores follow an AR(1) with
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. Innovations are }\DIFaddend Student-$t$\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{innovations, so they are autocorrelatedand heavy-tailed; a
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, and
the scores are autocorrelated. A }\DIFaddend log-scale Ornstein--Uhlenbeck \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{scene-activity }\DIFdelend process drives
the backbone's false-positive rate\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{and is deliberately }\emph{\DIFdel{not}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{part of }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, and it is absent from }\DIFaddend the operator's
\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{obvious
(clock, weather) taxonomy; dumping }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{clock-and-weather context. Dumping }\DIFaddend events are episodic\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{and recurring, with
a configurable }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{: a set }\DIFaddend number of episodes
per camera\DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, }\DIFaddend separated by exponential gaps, \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{only
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{with }\DIFaddend a fraction $\pi$ of \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{the frames
within an episode anomalous , and a ramp at each end; and episodes }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{frames
anomalous inside each episode. Episodes }\DIFaddend propagate along the camera graph with a
lag.
\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Defaults put the null score near $0.24$ and
an anomalous frame near $0.75$, i.e. \ a per-frame signal weak enough that it
must accumulate over time --- the regime where sequential methods earn their
keep. \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\Cref{sec:limitations} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
states plainly what this does and does not
establish}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin 

\DIFadd{The simulated day has \SimDiurnalFrames{} frames, which is \SimDiurnalMin{}\,min
at 25\,fps. This is the diurnal period in Experiments 1--12. Their streams last
40 to 60 minutes. A shorter horizon sweep, up to \HorizonMaxHours\,h, is in Online Resource~1,
Sect.~S3. Only the week-long experiment in \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:validity} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
uses a full
24\,h simulated day}\DIFaddend .

\paragraph{Baselines.}
A fixed score threshold\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{with an optional $k$-of-$m$ persistence filter; }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, and }\DIFaddend a per-frame threshold on the \DIFdelbegin \emph{\DIFdel{same}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{same }\DIFaddend conformal p-values
\DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{that }\DIFaddend SENTINEL-E consumes, which isolates \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{optional stopping from calibration; CUSUM \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{page1954}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
;
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{the stopping rule from calibration. The
others are CUSUM \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{page1954}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
, }\DIFaddend Shiryaev--Roberts \citep{roberts1966}\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{; }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, }\DIFaddend a
parametric e-detector \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{using the same
changepoint mixture }\DIFdelend with a Gaussian likelihood-ratio bet\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{instead of a conformal
one; }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, }\DIFaddend a reimplementation of
E-SHIFT \citep{khan2026eshift}\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{; and, as the ablation that
isolates the paper's core, the same pipeline with }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, and }\DIFaddend the changepoint mixture\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{in
place of the episodic one. That last baseline is not a straw man: by
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{prop:generalisation} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
it is a faithful }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. The changepoint
mixture is an }\DIFaddend instance of the \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{PFA-controlled
}\DIFdelend non-partitioned e-detector of
\citet{saha2026nonpartitioned} \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- a mixture over
start times with weights summing to one, under a geometric prior and a fixed
betting family rather than an infimum over a composite pre-change class --- so the episodes-versus-changepoints comparison in \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:episodes}
}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
is a
comparison against the current state of the art in }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{by \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{prop:generalisation}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
, so \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:episodes}
}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
compares against a published }\DIFaddend anytime-valid \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{change
detection, run through an identical }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{method under the same }\DIFaddend conformal front
end. \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Every baseline is
fitted on the same calibration setand evaluated on the same decorrelated
betting grid, so none is helped or penalised by the frame-rate choice}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{All methods share one calibration set}\DIFaddend .


Every method \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{also }\emph{\DIFdel{restarts}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{restarts }\DIFaddend after an alarm\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{. This matters more than it
sounds}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, as an operator does once an alert has been
investigated}\DIFaddend . Under the single-shot convention of \cref{def:tuv}\DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, }\DIFaddend a detector
stops at its first alarm \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, so a method that false-alarms early is disabled for the rest of
the stream }\DIFdelend and records a \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{missed detection for an eventit never got to see. That
convention is right for stating the guarantee and wrong for measuring delay,
because an operator investigates an alert and resumes monitoring}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{miss for every later event}\DIFaddend . Restarting
leaves the null metrics \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{untouched --- the probability of at least one false
alarm, }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{unchanged, because the false-alarm probability }\DIFaddend and the
time to the first \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{one, }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{alarm }\DIFaddend do not depend on what \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{happens afterwards
--- and can only help a method that cries wolf.
Under the single-shot convention
the classical baselines score miss rates near one; we report the restarting
numbers because they are the ones that give those baselines a fair hearing. Each is
swept over its own knob and plotted at its realised false-alarm rate. }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{follows.
}\DIFaddend 


\DIFdelbegin \paragraph{\DIFdel{Reproducibility.}}
%DIFAUXCMD
\addtocounter{paragraph}{-1}%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{Seeds are fixed in each script; }\texttt{\DIFdel{experiments/run\_all.py}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{reproduces every
number, and every }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Monte Carlo rates carry Wilson 95\% intervals. An upper limit of zero counts
gives the strongest statement that a zero count supports. Each experiment draws
its own seed range, so the same configuration in two tables is an independent
replicate. The paired comparisons in \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:episodes} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
share seeds within each
comparison. Every }\DIFaddend quantity quoted in \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{this }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{the }\DIFaddend text is generated into
\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{a macro file
from the experiment output, so a stale number is not possible. All timings }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \texttt{\DIFadd{results/tables/macros.tex}} \DIFadd{by }\texttt{\DIFadd{experiments/make\_macros.py}}\DIFadd{, which
reads the saved experiment output. Timings }\DIFaddend are single-thread CPU\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{on the machine that produced the statistical results}\DIFdelend .

\subsection{Real-video validation}
\label{sec:real-data}

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Every result in this subsection runs the identical, unmodified pipeline ---
the same conformal calibration, the same episodic e-process, the same
classical baselines --- on real per-frame scores rather than the
simulator .
Two classical video-anomaly-detection corpora supply continuous, real,
frame-labelled surveillance footage}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Real video tests whether the guarantee holds per video on footage that the
simulator does not produce. Three corpora supply it}\DIFaddend : CUHK Avenue
\citep{lu2013avenue}\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{(\RealAvenueNTrainVideos{} training / \RealAvenueNTestVideos{} testing
videos, a campus walkway, \RealAvenueNEpisodes{} distinct labelled anomalous
runs across the test split) and }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, }\DIFaddend UCSD Ped2 \citep{mahadevan2010ucsd} \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{(\RealPedTwoNTrainVideos{} training
/ \RealPedTwoNTestVideos{} testing videos, a
pedestrian walkway, \RealPedTwoNEpisodes{} runs, one per test video). Neither
ships per-frame detector scores, so we compute our own: Farneback dense
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{and Mivia-IWDD
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{greco2026iwdd}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
, an illegal-dumping corpus. Each corpus is run with two
frozen backbones, and no backbone is fine-tuned.
}

\paragraph{\DIFadd{Data and backbones.}} \DIFadd{Ped2 has \RealPedTwoNTrainVideos{} training
videos, all normal, and \RealPedTwoNTestVideos{} test videos from one fixed
camera. Avenue has \RealAvenueNTrainVideos{} training and
\RealAvenueNTestVideos{} test videos from one fixed camera. Mivia-IWDD has 200
training videos, all labelled negative, and 100 test videos: 50 negative, 25
static dumping and 25 dynamic dumping. Each Mivia video comes from a different
camera, so Mivia calibration pools cameras. The labels mark only the
dumping onset. Mivia is sampled at 10\,fps; the other two corpora at 25\,fps.
}

\DIFadd{The flow backbone is Farneback }\DIFaddend optical-flow magnitude \citep{farneback2003},
averaged per frame \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{at $64\times64$ resolution and median-filtered over three
frames within each video. The appearance backbone is PatchCore
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\citep{roth2022total} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
with a frozen WideResNet-50-2 pretrained on ImageNet.
It uses layers 2 }\DIFaddend and \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{denoised
by a 3-frame per-video median filter (disclosed below), reaching frame-level
ROC-AUC \RealPedTwoAuc{} on }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{3, a 1\% coreset of training patches, and the maximum
over patches of the nearest-neighbour distance as the frame score. Its frame
AUCs are \RThreePedTwoFlowAuc{} (}\DIFaddend Ped2 \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{and \RealAvenueAuc{} on Avenue --- a real, classical,
unsupervised backbonechosen for transparency over benchmark
leaderboard position; \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:related} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
situates it against 2025--2026
methods that reach substantially higher AUC on the same pair. Streams are
built by }\texttt{\DIFdel{build\_stream\_from\_clips}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{exactly as \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:problem}
}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
describes: calibration is
the official training split, and the deployment
stream is spliced from real labelled runs inside the test split, with no frame ever eligible for both roles. }%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < \begin{figure}[t]
%DIFDELCMD <   \centering
%DIFDELCMD <   \resultfig{fig_r0_qualitative}{}
%DIFDELCMD <   %%%
%DIFDELCMD < \caption{%
{%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdelFL{What the backbone actually sees. One normal and one anomalous
  frame from each real corpus, with the Farneback flow-magnitude field and
  the resulting scalar score computed at the identical 64$\times$64
  resolution the rest of this subsection runs on --- these are the real
  numbers the pipeline consumes, not an illustration of them. The Ped2
  anomaly is a cyclist among pedestrians; the Avenue anomaly is a loitering
  pedestrian. Purely for legibility in print, the }\emph{\DIFdelFL{frame}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdelFL{panels are
  re-read from each dataset's original higher-resolution release at the
  identical frame index; the flow field and the score never are. Notice how
  little the score moves between the two conditions
  (\QualExamplePedTwoNormal{}$\to$\QualExamplePedTwoAnomalous{} on Ped2,
  \QualExampleAvenueNormal{}$\to$\QualExampleAvenueAnomalous{} on Avenue) ---
  a concrete picture of the weak per-frame signal \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:intro} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
motivates
  accumulating sequentially rather than thresholding on its own.}}
  %DIFAUXCMD
%DIFDELCMD < \label{fig:real-qualitative}
%DIFDELCMD < \end{figure}
%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\paragraph{\DIFdel{A real train/test shift, disclosed and quantified.}} %DIFAUXCMD
\addtocounter{paragraph}{-1}%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{Before any
sequential machinery runs, a direct quantile check on the raw scores shows
that \RealPedTwoShiftFrac{}\% of }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{flow), \RThreePedTwoPCAuc{} (}\DIFaddend Ped2 \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{'s confirmed-normal test frames, and \RealAvenueShiftFrac{}\% of Avenue 's, exceed the 95th percentile of the }\emph{\DIFdel{official training}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{split's score distribution --- both far above the
5\% a well-calibrated split would show. Real footage across a train/test boundary is not, in this backbone's scores, }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{PatchCore),
\RThreeAvenueFlowAuc{} (Avenue flow), \RThreeAvenuePCAuc{} (Avenue PatchCore),
\RThreeMiviaFlowAuc{} (Mivia flow) and \RThreeMiviaPCAuc{} (Mivia PatchCore).
The Mivia flow score is below chance. PatchCore is a strong training-free appearance
backbone, but it is not the state of the art on these benchmarks. Its frame AUC is
0.828 on Ped2, 0.737 on Avenue (below flow's 0.783) and 0.588 on Mivia. The power
results below therefore bound what such frozen backbones deliver, not what the best
published video anomaly detectors would deliver.
}

\paragraph{\DIFadd{Protocol.}} \DIFadd{The unit of replication is }\DIFaddend the \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{same distribution twice; this
is a fact about the datasets and the backbone, not an artefact of splicing.
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{video. Each test video is
monitored alone by a freshly started detector, and no frame is reused between
calibration and monitoring. Calibration uses training videos only. For Ped2
and Avenue it uses all training videos of the same camera. For Mivia it uses
the training videos of the other folds, pooled across cameras.
}\DIFaddend 

\DIFdelbegin \paragraph{\DIFdel{Real autocorrelation, and why it is the headline finding here.}}
%DIFAUXCMD
\addtocounter{paragraph}{-1}%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\Cref{thm:beta} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
needs the
calibration draws to be close to i. i. d. , which is
why \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:layer1} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
thins the betting grid to the decorrelation lag }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Operating points are selected on training videos only, before any test video
is read. SENTINEL-E's betting lag $L^\ast(\alpha)$ is the smallest lag on the
grid $\{1,2,3,5,7,10,15,20,30,50\}$ whose leave-one-video-out (LOVO)
per-video false-alarm proportion is at most $\alpha$}\DIFaddend . \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{On the AR(1) simulator that lag is small and an anomalous episode lasts thousands of
frames, so thinning costs almost nothing. Real Farneback-flow scores behave
nothing like that: the
sample autocorrelation of the official training split
does not fall below 0.05 until lag \RealPedTwoFullLag{} on Ped2 and lag
\RealAvenueFullLag{} on Avenue --- two orders of magnitude past anything the simulator produced --- while a real labelled episode lasts a median of
\RealPedTwoEpiMedian{} frames on }\DIFdelend Ped2 \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{(\RealPedTwoEpiMin{}--\RealPedTwoEpiMax{}) and
just \RealAvenueEpiMedian{} on Avenue (\RealAvenueEpiMin{}--\RealAvenueEpiMax{}).
Thinning to the fully rigorous lag leaves at most one or two betting
opportunities inside a typical real event --- too few for any sequential test, at any $\alpha$, to detect reliably. }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{and Avenue use
LOVO over their 16 training videos. Mivia uses 10 folds by video index, each
holding out 20 training videos. Each classical baseline is thinned at the same
$L^\ast(\alpha)$. Its knob is the first admissible knob in sweep order, from the
most aggressive. A knob is admissible when its LOVO proportion is at most
$\alpha$. If no knob is admissible, the most conservative knob is used. This
happens for \RThreeBaselineNoKnob{} of the \RThreeBaselineConfigs{} baseline
configurations at $\alpha=0.1$, and those results describe a conservative
setting, not a tuned one.
}

\DIFadd{This replaces an earlier evaluation in which the betting lag was tuned on test
streams and normal frames were reused across spliced streams.
}

\DIFadd{A null trial is a video with no anomaly, or the pre-onset part of an anomalous
video that lasts at least 2\,s. On Mivia the null trials are the 50 negative
test videos. A detection is an alarm inside an episode window, extended by a
one-second grace period. The detector restarts after each alarm, so a video
can contribute more than one alarm to the rate per hour. It counts once in the
per-video proportion. All proportions use exact Clopper--Pearson intervals.
False alarms per hour use an exact Poisson interval. The effective sample size
is stated with each result: the number of null videos, and the number of
episodes. Avenue has 74 episodes in 21 videos, so its detection intervals treat
episodes as independent and are optimistic.
}

\paragraph{\DIFadd{Results.}} \DIFadd{\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\Cref{tab:r3_selection} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
gives the selected lags, and
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{tab:r3_test} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
the test results at $\alpha=0.1$. \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\Cref{fig:r3_per_video}
}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
plots each method's test false alarms per hour against its detection rate.
}

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\Cref{tab:real_lag_sweep} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
reports that crossing directly. Rather than pick
the one lag that works best and report only it, every lag swept is shown,
because the crossing itself --- not the single surviving operating point ---
is the finding : real per-frame anomaly scores do not leave a comfortable
region in which both validity}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{The first finding concerns validity. On Ped2 and Avenue the false-alarm counts at
$\alpha=0.1$ are small. The null footage is short: 12.6\,s for Ped2 (three pre-onset
segments), 6.9\,min for Avenue (16 videos) }\DIFaddend and \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{power hold simultaneously, the way the simulated AR(1) streams did throughout \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:validity,sec:episodes}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
. The lag this sweep selects (\RealPedTwoLag{} on }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{26.9\,min for Mivia (50 negative
videos). At this length any outcome is consistent with the nominal level. }\DIFaddend Ped2 \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, \RealAvenueLag{} on Avenue )
is fixed once, from this diagnostic alone, and reused unchanged for every
other real-data result in this subsection --- including the classical
baselines in \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{tab:real_delay_far}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
, which are thinned to the identical
grid so that no method is favoured by a different frame rate. }%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < \inputtable{tabr1_lag_sweep}
%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\Cref{tab:real_validity} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
sweeps $\alpha$ at that fixed lag}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{flow has \RThreePedTwoFlowFAk{}/\RThreePedTwoFlowFAn{} false
alarms, Ped2 PatchCore has \RThreePedTwoPCFAk{}/\RThreePedTwoPCFAn{}, Avenue
flow has \RThreeAvenueFlowFAk{}/\RThreeAvenueFlowFAn{} and Avenue PatchCore has
\RThreeAvenuePCFAk{}/\RThreeAvenuePCFAn{}. The intervals are wide}\DIFaddend . Ped2 \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{controls its false-alarm rate at \RealPedTwoPfaHeadline{}
(95\% CI upper end \RealPedTwoCiHiHeadline{}) at
$\alpha=0.1$, with roughly two
thirds of real spliced events detected (miss rate \RealPedTwoMissHeadline{}) --- a
real, modest,
but genuine detection window. Avenue is harder: its shorter, sparser real episodes leave a realised PFA of \RealAvenuePfaHeadline{} at the same
$\alpha$, but a miss rate of
\RealAvenueMissHeadline{}.
At the loosest level swept, $\alpha=\RealAvenueWorstAlpha$, Avenue's realised rate (\RealAvenueWorstPfa{})
still does not exceed the nominal level, so the guarantee itself is not
violated anywhere on this grid; what real footage costs Avenue is power, not
validity. This is not the failure the
simulated experiments were built to
surface, }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{flow at
$\alpha=0.2$ has 3 of 3 false alarms, with selected lag 1 (Online Resource~1, Table~S1),
so the Ped2 result depends on the level chosen. Mivia flow has
\RThreeMiviaFlowFAk{}/\RThreeMiviaFlowFAn{} false alarms, inside the level.
Mivia PatchCore has \RThreeMiviaPCFAk{}/\RThreeMiviaPCFAn{}, a proportion of
\RThreeMiviaPCFAp{} with interval $[\RThreeMiviaPCFALo{},\RThreeMiviaPCFAHi{}]$.
This exceeds $0.1$ at the point estimate, and the interval contains $0.1$. The
one-sided exact binomial probability of at least nine false alarms in 50 videos at
$p=0.1$ is $0.058$, so the excess is not significant at the 5\% level. The count also
moves with $\alpha$: it is 3 of 50 at $\alpha=0.05$ }\DIFaddend and \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{it could not have been --- it is specific to what real
optical-flow scores from real short-duration events actually look like, and \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:limitations} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
returns to it as the paper's central open problem
rather than a number to look past}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{12 of 50 at $\alpha=0.2$
(full sweep in Online Resource~1, Sect.~S1). The excess has several candidate causes,
and 50 null videos cannot separate them. First, Mivia calibrates across cameras, but
the guarantee is per camera, and every test camera is unseen, so the per-camera
premise is not tested here. Second, the p-values are serially dependent (the
paragraph below). Third, the lag and the alarm boundary are chosen on training videos.
The selected lag is the smallest one whose LOVO proportion is at most $\alpha$. For
Mivia PatchCore, $L^\ast=7$ has 20 of 200 $=0.100$, exactly at $\alpha$, and selection at
a boundary can pick a lag whose true rate lies above $\alpha$}\DIFaddend .

\DIFdelbegin %DIFDELCMD < \inputtable{tabr1b_validity}
%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\paragraph{\DIFdel{Classical baselines on the same real streams.}}
%DIFAUXCMD
\addtocounter{paragraph}{-1}%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\Cref{tab:real_delay_far} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
repeats the delay-vs-false-alarm-rate comparison of \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:delay} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
on real video, with every method --- SENTINEL-E, a fixed
threshold, the per-frame p-value threshold, CUSUM, Shiryaev--Roberts, a
parametric e-detector, and E-SHIFT --- thinned to the identical fixed lag
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{tab:real_lag_sweep} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
selects for that dataset.
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Per-video rates understate what an operator sees, because the detector restarts after
each alarm. Per hour of null footage, }\DIFaddend SENTINEL-E \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{is the only
method that reaches the target false-alarm probability while still detecting
most events on either dataset (\RealDelayPedTwoNBaselinesPass{} of \RealDelayPedTwoNBaselines{} classical baselines pass on }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{raises 284.8 false alarms on }\DIFaddend Ped2
\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{,
\RealDelayAvenueNBaselinesPass{} of \RealDelayAvenueNBaselines{} on Avenue ). The per-frame p-value threshold --- consuming the identical conformal
p-values }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{flow, from one alarm in 12.6\,s. On Avenue PatchCore it raises 86.6 per hour, and all
ten of its alarms come from one null video in 6.9\,min. On Mivia PatchCore it raises
100.5 per hour, with 45 alarms across nine videos in 26.9\,min. A per-video
proportion counts a video once, however many alarms it carries. The hourly figures
rest on very short null footage and are illustrations, not operating rates.
}

\DIFadd{The second finding concerns power, which depends strongly on the backbone.
Ped2 flow detects \RThreePedTwoFlowDetk{} of \RThreePedTwoFlowDetn{} episodes
and Ped2 PatchCore detects \RThreePedTwoPCDetk{} of \RThreePedTwoPCDetn{}.
Avenue detects only \RThreeAvenueFlowDetk{} of \RThreeAvenueFlowDetn{} with
flow and \RThreeAvenuePCDetk{} of \RThreeAvenuePCDetn{} with PatchCore. Mivia
flow detects none of its \RThreeMiviaFlowDetn{} positive videos. Mivia
PatchCore detects \RThreeMiviaPCDetk{} of \RThreeMiviaPCDetn{}. Its median
delay is \RThreeMiviaPCDelayMed{}\,s after onset.
}

\DIFadd{The third finding concerns the baselines, and it does not favour }\DIFaddend SENTINEL-E\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{does, differing only in the stopping rule --- never fires
at any swept threshold on either dataset: no single real frame's score is
extreme enough on its own to cross even a lenient per-frame significance
level, which is exactly the argument \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:episodic} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
makes for accumulating
evidence sequentially rather than thresholding one frame at a time, now
confirmed on real scores rather than simulated ones. The classical
parametric procedures }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. On
Mivia PatchCore every baseline also exceeds $0.1$ on test, with 9 to 18 of 50
negative videos raising an alarm. On Avenue PatchCore, three of the six baselines
}\DIFaddend (CUSUM, Shiryaev--Roberts \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{and }\DIFaddend the parametric e-detector) \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{reach a false-alarm probability near one at
essentially every settingof
their own tuning knob on real footage, consistent with
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:delay}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
's
simulated finding and for the same underlying reason: their pre-change model
is a poor fit to real, heavy-tailed, strongly autocorrelated detector output. }%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < \begin{figure}[t]
%DIFDELCMD <   \centering
%DIFDELCMD <   \resultfig{fig_r2_delay_far}{}
%DIFDELCMD <   %%%
%DIFDELCMD < \caption{%
{%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdelFL{Detection delay against realised false-alarm probability on real
  video (\RealDelayReps{} null and signal streams per operating point), at
  each dataset's fixed operating lag from \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{fig:real-validity}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
. Both axes
  are log-scaled: every classical baseline's knob sweep saturates at, or
  near, a false-alarm probability of one on real footage, so without a log
  delay axis their whole operating range collapses into an illegible cluster
  against SENTINEL-E's much larger delays. Points are additionally jittered
  horizontally by a small, fixed, method-specific, never-positive offset
  (at most 3\%, disclosed in }\texttt{\DIFdelFL{exp\_r2\_delayfar.py}}%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdelFL{) purely so
  distinct methods' markers at the same realised PFA do not print on top of
  one another; no offset changes which side of any false-alarm threshold a
  point falls on. Curves that never reach the plotted range detect too
  rarely, on real spliced events, at any realised false-alarm rate their
  swept knob achieves.}}
  %DIFAUXCMD
%DIFDELCMD < \label{fig:real-delayfar}
%DIFDELCMD < \end{figure}
%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < \inputtable{tabr2_delay_far}
%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\paragraph{\DIFdel{What this subsection does and does not establish.}} %DIFAUXCMD
\addtocounter{paragraph}{-1}%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{It establishes
that the pipeline runs end to end on real , unmodified video--- real
backbone, real train/test split, real spliced events, real classical
baselines --- and that }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{had no admissible knob at
$\alpha=0.1$ and used the most conservative setting. Across all 36 baseline
configurations at this level, 15 had none. The fixed threshold and E-SHIFT detect
more episodes than SENTINEL-E on Avenue PatchCore (15 and 29 of 74, against 9), with
2 and 4 false alarms in 16 videos. On Mivia PatchCore, CUSUM matches the 9 false
alarms in 50 videos, detects more episodes (11 of 50 against 10) and has a lower rate
per hour (53.6 against 100.5). Its knob is the most conservative in its sweep, so
this is not a tuned baseline. On Ped2 PatchCore the fixed threshold detects 8 of 12
episodes against 7 of 12, with no false alarm in three null videos, which is too
little evidence to rule out an excess. }\DIFaddend SENTINEL-E \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{'s time-uniform guarantee holds on both
corpora at the lag each one's own diagnostic selects, which the naive
classical alternatives do not manage on either. It does not establish that
this backbone, at this frame rate, is adequate for deployment: Avenue's
power is weak, the horizon tested is minutes rather than weeks, and the two
datasets are pedestrian-walkway anomaly corpora, not illegal-dumping
footage, because no continuously-labelled real dumping corpus of the
required length is publicly available (\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:limitations}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
). What
transfers is }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{therefore shows no demonstrated
advantage over these baselines on real video. Its claimed advantage is the
horizon-free guarantee, which the short real videos cannot exercise.
}

\paragraph{\DIFadd{Dependence.}} \DIFadd{The guarantee rests on }\DIFaddend the \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{pipeline and the diagnostic , not the specific power number:
a stronger backbone with genuinely independent frame-to-frame errors, run
through the identical calibration and
episodic layers, is }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{thinned p-values, so
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{tab:r3_dependence} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
measures their dependence. Two quantities are
reported. The pooled autocorrelation of the concatenated training scores
exceeds $0.05$ at every lag up to 200. That length reflects differences
between the mean scores of videos, not the time scale of the process, and
it is not used. The within-video autocorrelation is }\DIFaddend the \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{direction most
likely to close the gap \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{tab:real_lag_sweep} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
exposes}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{relevant quantity. At
lag one it averages \RThreeWithinLagOneMin{} to \RThreeWithinLagOneMax{}. At
lag 25 it averages between essentially zero and \RThreeWithinLagTwentyFiveMax{}.
Lag 25 is 1\,s at 25\,fps and 2.5\,s at 10\,fps.
}

\DIFadd{The thinned p-values remain positively correlated. At the selected lags the
lag-one correlation ranges from \RThreeDepCorrMin{} to \RThreeDepCorrMax{}. The
empirical CDF shows no excess of small values. The largest positive deviation
is \RThreeDepSupMax{} at the selected lags, and \RThreeDepSupOneMax{} at lag one.
The conditional-mean diagnostic contradicts conditional super-uniformity. If $p_t$
is conditionally super-uniform given the past, its mean given $p_{t-1}\le0.1$ is at
least $0.5$. The observed means lie between \RThreeDepCondMin{} and
\RThreeDepCondMax{}, on \RThreeDepCountMin{} to \RThreeDepCountMax{} pairs. A small
p-value is followed by another small one. The hypothesis of \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{thm:episodic}
}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
therefore fails on these thinned streams, and real-video validity can only be
established empirically. The observable consequence is the per-video false-alarm
rate. On Ped2 and Avenue it is consistent with $0.1$, but the null footage is short.
On Mivia PatchCore it exceeds $0.1$}\DIFaddend .

\DIFdelbegin \subsection{\DIFdel{A single stream, worked through}}
%DIFAUXCMD
\addtocounter{subsection}{-1}%DIFAUXCMD
%DIFDELCMD < \label{sec:worked-example}
%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \inputtable{tabr3_dependence}
\DIFaddend 

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Every quantity in the sections that follow is an aggregate over hundreds of simulated streams. Before reporting any of them, it is
worth looking at one
stream in full, because the abstract objects in \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:method} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
--- a
p-value, a betting factor, accumulated wealth, an alarm --- are otherwise easy
to read only as symbols}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \paragraph{\DIFadd{Action or residue.}} \DIFadd{The Mivia labels mark only the onset. They do
not say whether the detector responds to the dumping action or to the waste
left behind. Flow measures motion. Flow scores are higher on negative videos
than before onset in positive videos, so flow can at best respond to the
action, and the motion of other activity in the scene confuses it. Appearance
can in principle see deposited waste. Under PatchCore, static dumping is
detected in \RThreeMiviaPCStaticDetk{} of \RThreeMiviaPCStaticDetn{} videos and
dynamic dumping in \RThreeMiviaPCDynDetk{} of \RThreeMiviaPCDynDetn{}. These
counts do not differ at this sample size. The per-type split for all methods
is in Online Resource 1, Sect.~S1. A residue that persists after the action is
the permanent-change case, in which the episode never ends, $\eta=0$. The
episodic mixture contains that case. A dedicated residue model would change
the background model itself, and we leave it to future work.
}

\paragraph{\DIFadd{Scope.}} \DIFadd{The real test videos last seconds to about a minute. The real data therefore bear only weakly on per-video false-alarm control, because
the null footage is short. The long-horizon
claim rests on the simulated streams in \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:validity}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
, which run for up
to a week of simulated time}\DIFaddend .

\begin{figure}[t]
  \centering
  \DIFdelbeginFL %DIFDELCMD < \resultfig{fig6_worked_example}{}
%DIFDELCMD <   %%%
\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \resultfig{fig_r0_qualitative}{}
  \DIFaddendFL \caption{\DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{One simulated \WorkedT{}-second stream with \WorkedNEpisodes{}
  episodes (shaded)}\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \DIFaddFL{Real frames}\DIFaddendFL , \DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{chosen for its episode count rather than for how
  favourably it behaves}\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \DIFaddFL{real scores}\DIFaddendFL . \DIFdelbeginFL \textbf{\DIFdelFL{(a)}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \DIFaddFL{Ped2 and Avenue rows: one normal and one
  anomalous frame, with }\DIFaddendFL the \DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{raw backbone }\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \DIFaddFL{Farneback flow-magnitude field and its }\DIFaddendFL score, \DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{spiking during
  each episode but also, by construction, during ordinary null activity.
  }\textbf{\DIFdelFL{(b)}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdelFL{the conformal p-value }\DIFdelendFL at
  \DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{each betting instant (log scale);
  gaps are frames }\DIFdelendFL the \DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{decorrelation lag skips or }\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \DIFaddFL{$64\times64$ resolution }\DIFaddendFL the \DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{calibration abstains on}\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \DIFaddFL{pipeline uses}\DIFaddendFL . \DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{Notice that the score spikes in (a) do not translate into uniformly small
  p-values in (b) --- the score distribution the conformal layer calibrates
  against already absorbs much of }\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \DIFaddFL{Mivia rows: }\DIFaddendFL the \DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{diurnal }\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \DIFaddFL{static }\DIFaddendFL and
  \DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{weather-driven variation}\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \DIFaddFL{dynamic test videos}\DIFaddendFL , \DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{so
  only }\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \DIFaddFL{each with one pre-onset frame, one post-onset frame and
  }\DIFaddendFL the \DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{residual that context cannot explain shows up as evidence}\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \DIFaddFL{PatchCore score over time}\DIFaddendFL . \DIFdelbeginFL \textbf{\DIFdelFL{(c)}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \DIFaddFL{Each video is }\DIFaddendFL the \DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{episodic e-process'}\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \DIFaddFL{first positive test video of
  its type, in video-id order, with at least 3\,}\DIFaddendFL s \DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{accumulated log-wealth}\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \DIFaddFL{before onset. It was not
  chosen on its score}\DIFaddendFL . The \DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{dash-dot
  }\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \DIFaddFL{dashed }\DIFaddendFL line \DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{is the labelled }\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \DIFaddFL{marks }\DIFaddendFL onset\DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{; the process is already drifting down slightly
  beforehand (an ordinary null fluctuation}\DIFdelendFL , \DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{licensed by }\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \DIFaddFL{and }\DIFaddendFL the \DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{supermartingale
  property)}\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \DIFaddFL{coloured points mark
  the two frames. The frame panels are shown at display resolution}\DIFaddendFL , \DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{climbs sharply once }\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \DIFaddFL{from }\DIFaddendFL the
  \DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{first episode's evidence accumulates}\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \DIFaddFL{original releases. The Mivia release is not redistributed}\DIFaddendFL , \DIFaddbeginFL \DIFaddFL{so only downscaled
  stills are stored. The flow }\DIFaddendFL and \DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{crosses $\log(1/\alpha)$ \WorkedDelaySec{}\,s after onset --- }\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \DIFaddFL{score panels are computed from }\DIFaddendFL the \DIFdelbeginFL \DIFdelFL{red point}\DIFdelendFL \DIFaddbeginFL \DIFaddFL{pipeline

\DIFaddendFL \end{figure}

\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{This particular stream isnot cherry-picked for a fast detection: it }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{The qualitative panels show how small the per-frame signal is. Ped2 moves from
\QualExamplePedTwoNormal{} to \QualExamplePedTwoAnomalous{}, and Avenue moves
from \QualExampleAvenueNormal{} to \QualExampleAvenueAnomalous{}. These are
the values the pipeline consumes, and they explain why the method accumulates
evidence over time.
}


\subsection{\DIFadd{A single stream, worked through}}
\label{sec:worked-example}

\DIFadd{Aggregates over hundreds of streams are hard to read without one stream in mind.
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\Cref{fig:worked-example} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
shows a \WorkedT{}-second stream with
\WorkedNEpisodes{} episodes. It }\DIFaddend is the first seed tried at this configuration,
\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{and its three episodes are placed at
$\WorkedEp{}$. What it shows, concretely, is the mechanism the rest of the
paper measures in aggregate.
The wealth process spends }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{not one chosen for a fast detection.
}

\begin{figure}[t]
  \centering
  \resultfig{fig6_worked_example}{}
  \caption{\DIFaddFL{One simulated \WorkedT{}-second stream with \WorkedNEpisodes{}
  episodes (shaded). }\textbf{\DIFaddFL{(a)}} \DIFaddFL{The raw backbone score. }\textbf{\DIFaddFL{(b)}} \DIFaddFL{The
  conformal p-value at each betting instant (log scale). Score spikes do not map
  onto uniformly small p-values, because the conformal layer absorbs much of the
  diurnal and weather variation. }\textbf{\DIFaddFL{(c)}} \DIFaddFL{The episodic log-wealth. The
  dash-dot line marks the labelled onset, and the red point marks the alarm at
  $\log(1/\alpha)$, \WorkedDelaySec{}\,s after onset.}}
  \label{fig:worked-example}
\end{figure}

\DIFadd{The wealth stays close to its starting value for }\DIFaddend most of the stream\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{essentially flat --- a slow, licensed drift rather than a trend, since
$(W_t)$ }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. Under the
null it }\DIFaddend is a supermartingale\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{under the null and }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, so it drifts but }\DIFaddend has no reason to grow\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- and
climbs by more than four orders of magnitude in the roughly \WorkedClimbSec\,s
it takes }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. Once }\DIFaddend the
first episode's evidence \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{to accumulate. After the alarm , further
episodes in this stream do not
visibly }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{accumulates, the wealth climbs to the alarm level
$1/\alpha=100$, \WorkedDelaySec{}\,s after onset. The later episodes do not
}\DIFaddend move the trace\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{: the wealth has already
crossed threshold and, in this }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, because this is a }\DIFaddend single-alarm illustration\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, the detector is not
restarted,
which is exactly the deployment convention (restart on alarm,
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:protocol}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
) that the Monte Carlo experiments use and this one figure
does not.
A reader who wants to see the restarted, }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. The restarted,
}\DIFaddend multi-alarm \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{behaviour on
this same stream, or to regenerate this figure on a different seed, can do so

}\DIFaddend \texttt{experiments/exp11\_worked\_example.py} \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, which costs one stream
simulation and one pipeline run}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{regenerates the figure for

shows that even where the }\emph{\DIFdel{stream}}%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{-level
miss rate is zero, the majority of individual episodes within that stream are
still missed, which is the mechanism this section's comparison is silent
about. }%DIFDELCMD <

\DIFdel{The
crossover itself is the signature \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:episodic} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
predicts: it appears

checks directly}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. With 1600-frame episodes
at $\pi=0.2$ and a gap of $12{,}000$ frames, the episodic arm is ahead by
\CornerLongGapDiff\,s (CI \CornerLongGapCi), with \CornerLongGapEpiOnly{}
discordant misses and none in reverse. With a gap of $1500$ frames the delay
advantage is \CornerShortGapDiff\,s (CI \CornerShortGapCi), but three discordant
misses give McNemar $p=\CornerShortGapP$, so the miss difference is not resolved.
Dense episodes ($\pi=0.7$) and a permanent change have delays of 9 to 12\,s. The
changepoint mixture is faster by \CornerDenseAbsDiff\,s in both cases. The 95\%
interval of that difference lies within 0.2\,s of zero, so both rows are equivalent
under the 1\,s margin and the difference is negligible. A single episode of 800
frames favours the changepoint mixture by \CornerSingleAbsDiff\,s (CI
\CornerSingleCi). Short episodes of 200 frames are missed by both mixtures at
$\alpha=0.01$, a miss rate of \CornerFloorMiss{}, which marks the detection floor
for that event length}\DIFaddend .

\DIFdelbegin \subsection{\DIFdel{What the mechanism actually explains}}
%DIFAUXCMD
\addtocounter{subsection}{-1}%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdelend \label{sec:per-episode}
\DIFdelbegin %DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < \inputtable{tab14_per_episode}
%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{Every result so far scores a }\emph{\DIFdel{stream}}%DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{: a run counts as detected once any
alarm follows the first onset, so a detector that sleeps through episodes $1$
through $k-1$ and only fires on episode $k$ is scored a clean detection. That
is not a hypothetical --- it is what happens here}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{Stream-level scores can hide episode-level misses}\DIFaddend . \Cref{tab:per_episode} scores
\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{every }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{each }\DIFaddend episode's own \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{detection windowindependently (}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{window, using }\DIFaddend \ExpTenReps{} streams per row\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{) and the two columns diverge sharply: at \EpiMaxCount{} }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. At four }\DIFaddend episodes the
stream miss rate is \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{\PerEpiManyEpiStreamMiss{} for the episodic mixture, matching the
``misses none at all'' language earlier in this section, while the }\emph{\DIFdel{per-episode}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{miss rate over the same streams is
\PerEpiManyEpiMiss{} ---
the vast majority of individual episodes are never flagged inside their own
window. The stream-level statistic is not wrong, but it answers a narrower
question than ``does the detector catch dumping episodes'': it answers ``does
enough evidence eventually accumulate somewhere in the stream'', and with
\EpiMaxCount{} chances per stream, a per-episode catch rate as low as
$1 - \PerEpiManyEpiMiss{} \approx 0.13$ is compatible with a near-zero }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{\PerEpiMidEpiStreamMiss{}, but the per-episode miss rate is
\PerEpiMidEpiMiss{}. At eight episodes, the }\DIFaddend stream miss rate \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{.
}%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{The more surprising finding is
that this }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{is
\PerEpiManyEpiStreamMiss{} and the }\DIFaddend per-episode \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{miss rate is not where the
episodic mixture's advantage lives.
At every episode count the episodic and
changepoint mixtures have statistically }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{rate is \PerEpiManyEpiMiss{}.
The two mixtures have }\DIFaddend indistinguishable per-episode miss rates \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{--- \PerEpiOneEpiMiss{} against \PerEpiOneCpMiss{} at one episode,
}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{at every count:
}\DIFaddend \PerEpiMidEpiMiss{} against \PerEpiMidCpMiss{} at \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{\EpiMidCount, and
\PerEpiManyEpiMiss{} against \PerEpiManyCpMiss{} at \EpiMaxCount. Neither
construction reliably resolves a single \EpiEventLenSec{}-second episode inside
its own narrow window; what differs between them, and what
\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{tab:paired_episodes} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
establishes with a paired test, is }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{four episodes. The difference
lies in }\DIFaddend how quickly evidence that does not resolve \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{within one episode
's window compounds toward
the first stream-level alarm. The changepoint mixture carries a channel's worth
of burned wealth from a reverted bet into the next episode; the episodic
process's quiet state does not.
That shows up as a shorter censored delay and,
at two and four episodes, as more streams resolved before the horizon --- both
already established in \mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:episodes} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
--- but it is a claim about how fast
evidence accumulates }\emph{\DIFdel{across}} %DIFAUXCMD
\DIFdel{a stream's episodes taken together, not a
claim that the episodic mixture independently catches more of them one at a time. The contributions list and abstract should be read with that scope. }%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{We report the single-episode row rather than omitting it. A strict
generalisation should cost something where its extra structure is absent, and
quantifying that cost is what makes the rest of the table trustworthy.
}%DIFDELCMD < 

%DIFDELCMD < %%%
\DIFdel{\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\Cref{tab:intermittency} }\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
varies the fraction $\pi$ of episode frames that show
the act, at \EpiBestCount{} episodes and with both mixtures using the same
undilated grid (\mbox{%DIFAUXCMD
\cref{sec:dilation}}\hskip0pt%DIFAUXCMD
) }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{inside one episode
accumulates across the stream. The paired tests above measure that, not
independent catches of each episode}\DIFaddend .
\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{Down to $\pi = 0.5$ the task is easy
enough that the two are indistinguishable and neither misses anything; the
separation appears only at $\pi = \EpiMinPi$, where the episodes become weak
enough that several are needed before the evidence crosses the threshold ---
which is , again, precisely when accumulating across episodes is what matters. Below $\pi \approx 0.2$ neither variant detects reliably at any setting we
tried, and we take that as the honest edge of the operating envelope rather than
a tuning problem. }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin

\DIFaddend

Each $\pm$ is 1.96 standard errors. At $\alpha=0.2$ the two tie: }\DIFaddend SENTINEL-E \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{detects in \DelaySentinelSec}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{has a
censored delay of \EqSEFifthDelaySec}\DIFaddend \,s (\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{$\pm$}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{$\pm\EqSEFifthCiSec$) and the changepoint
mixture \EqCpFifthDelaySec}\DIFaddend \,\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{\DelaySentinelCi}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{s. At $\alpha=0.1$ the changepoint mixture is faster,
at 138.1\,s against 146.4\,s for SENTINEL-E, with intervals of about $\pm52$\,s for
each. At $\alpha=0.05$ the figures are 181.4\,s for the changepoint mixture and
171.2\,s for SENTINEL-E. At $\alpha=0.01$, SENTINEL-E is faster, at
\EqSEHundredthDelaySec\,s against \EqCpHundredthDelaySec\,s, with miss rate
\EqSEHundredthMiss{} against \EqCpHundredthMiss. The ordering therefore changes
with $\alpha$, and the differences at $\alpha=0.2$ and $0.1$ are small against
their intervals. The nominal level
$\alpha=0.5$ is not a usable operating point. Its bound allows a false alarm on
half of all streams, so the low delay it reports (\EqSEHalfDelaySec}\DIFaddend \,s) \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{at a realised \DelaySentinelPfa, missing
\DelaySentinelMiss{} of events}\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{says
little about a usable threshold.
}

\DIFadd{The baselines are different}\DIFaddend . \DelayNumInadmissible{} of \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{the baselines }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{them }\DIFaddend never reach a
realised false-alarm probability of $0.05$ at any knob setting while still
detecting events (\DelayInadmissibleList)\DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{, which is the substantive
finding: on }\DIFdelend \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{. On }\DIFaddend streams with drift, heavy tails and
autocorrelation, \DIFdelbegin \DIFdel{the classical
procedures cannot be tuned into the operating region at all, because the
assumptions }\DIFdelend their thresholds rest on \DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{assumptions that }\DIFaddend do not hold\DIFaddbegin \DIFadd{, so they
cannot be tuned into the operating region}\DIFaddend .

: at \ExpThreeReps{} replicates per
